The 11 (12 counting `CACHE_LONG_SHORT_WRITES_ENABLED`) write/execution gates this bullet originally listed — `AI_GENERATION_API_ENABLED`, `AUTOMATION_WRITES_ENABLED`, `BROKERAGE_REFRESH_ENABLED`, `CACHE_LONG_SHORT_WRITES_ENABLED`, `COMMAND_EXECUTION_ENABLED`, `DEAD_LETTER_RETRY_ENABLED`, `GENERAL_SETTINGS_WRITES_ENABLED`, `LLM_WRITES_ENABLED`, `MACRO_GATE_WRITES_ENABLED`, `PROMPT_REGISTRY_WRITES_ENABLED`, `RAG_QUERY_API_ENABLED`, `STRATEGY_WRITES_ENABLED` — predate this decision and their **default values** are unaffected by it; do not flip their defaults without being explicitly asked to. **Update 2026-08-07**: the operator explicitly asked to flip the frozen 12 (now 16, folding in `PILOTS_API_ENABLED`/`JOBS_API_ENABLED`/`UNIVERSE_SYNC_ENABLED`/`AGENTIC_DISCOVERY_ENABLED`), superseding "do not flip without being explicitly asked" for exactly this named list, and added a new Feature Flags screen + registry to prevent silent fail-closed behavior. **This is orthogonal to the 2026-08-08 GUI-writability policy generalization documented in the bullet above**: that change moved these same 12 flags into `ALLOWED_KEYS` (a GUI-*visibility* change, `DANGEROUS_KEYS`-confirmation-gated), it did not touch any default value. The defaults were independently changed to True by the 2026-08-07 update. A *new* admin/API write or execution capability built from this date forward should ship active by default instead of behind a freshly-added opt-in flag. This does **not** extend to feature flags that change trading behavior (new signal modules, sizing changes, new data sources, forecasting changes) — those keep defaulting `False`/opt-in exactly as every "New settings default to today's exact behavior" bullet elsewhere in this file establishes, since a silent behavior change on `git pull` is a real risk for a live capital account. See `AGENTS.md`'s "Safety posture" section for the canonical statement of this policy.

- **Settings Reference & Dictionary (`GET`+`PUT`/`PATCH /settings/reference`, 2026-09)**: Exposes a complete, searchable dictionary of all 464 `settings.py` fields partitioned across 14 functional domains defined in `pilots/settings_domains.py`. Backs the Pilots PWA's Settings Reference screen (`/settings/reference`, `webapp/src/screens/SettingsReference.tsx`), accessible from the Modules & Integrations screen. Masks secrets as `"•••• (set)"` / `"(not set)"` for BOTH the live `value` and the field's compile-time `default`, surfaces runtime liveness metadata (`applies`, `restart_required`, `capture_sites`), and maps each key to its corresponding scoped settings editor URL or general tunables (`editable_at`), with direct "Edit here →" navigation. Backfilled 22 missing `Field(description=...)` entries in `settings.py` so all 464 fields have human-readable explanations. **Universal boolean toggle (`PUT`/`PATCH /settings/reference`, added same day)**: every non-secret, non-`no_op` BOOLEAN field (not just the ones a dedicated editor happens to also cover) renders a real `Toggle` directly on this screen and writes through a new `_REFERENCE_WRITE_INDEX` — **derived** from live introspection (`Settings.model_fields` ∩ `env_io.ALLOWED_KEYS`, boolean-typed, minus `docs/settings_liveness.json`'s `no_op` bucket), never hand-listed, so a brand-new boolean field becomes toggleable with zero code change here. Reuses the exact same shared `_validate_and_write_payload()` every other `/settings/*` editor already uses — the identical `DANGEROUS_KEYS` typed-confirmation gate (a lighter single-field `ReferenceDangerousConfirmDialog` in `SettingsReference.tsx`, sibling to `GenericSettingsEditor.tsx`'s batch-oriented `DangerousConfirmDialog`), the same bootstrap-key live-apply refusal, the same per-key liveness reporting. A `no_op` field (e.g. `OPTIONS_EARNINGS_CRUSH_ENABLED`) is deliberately excluded from the write index and reports `writable: false` — a live-looking Toggle for a field that provably does nothing on write would be CONSTRAINT #4-adjacent (a fabricated implication of effect, not a fabricated value). Promoted two new tunable groups to `_TUNABLE_GROUPS` in `api/pilots_api.py`: "Options Desk Automation" (13 fields) and "Circuit Breaker" (6 fields), plus 6 fields promoted into "Financial Constants" and "Runtime & Ops", bringing `_TUNABLE_INDEX` to 114 fields (was 89). (2026-09: the "Circuit Breaker" group was removed again when the dynamic circuit breaker was unwired, so `_TUNABLE_INDEX` is now 108 and `CIRCUIT_BREAKER_ENABLED` is a `no_op` field with no Toggle.) Protects against promoting dead/no-op keys (`OPTIONS_EARNINGS_CRUSH_ENABLED` is confirmed `no_op` and excluded from both `_TUNABLE_GROUPS` and `_REFERENCE_WRITE_INDEX`). Added `MULTI_BROKER_GATEWAY_ENABLED` to `pilots/feature_flags.py::WRITE_GATE_REASONS` and fixed feature flag mock parity in `webapp/src/api/mock.ts` (11 missing flags + `MULTI_BROKER_GATEWAY_ENABLED`; also fixed two incorrect hardcoded `editable_at` values for `ADVISORY_ONLY`/`BROKER_BACKEND` — both are listed in more than one editor's group, and the mock had picked the wrong one relative to `_build_editable_at_index()`'s real editor-precedence order). Fixed 7 stale/fabricated keys in `pilots/settings_domains.py`'s `_OVERRIDE_DOMAINS` (e.g. `KILLSWITCH_VIX_THRESHOLD` instead of the real `KILLSWITCH_VIX_THRESHOLD_AGREED`, a fabricated `DB_ECHO`/`SQLITE_*`/`MCP_OAUTH_USERS`) — harmless in practice (the lookup only ever queries real field names) but incorrect, now guarded by a hygiene test. Wired up `TabGuide` on all 8 settings wrapper screens (`tabGuideKey`) and added glossary entries (`"liveness"`, `"settings reference"`) and 8 `TAB_HELP` entries in `helpContent.ts`. **6-agent audit pass, same day**: found and fixed the missing write endpoint above (the original PR shipped read-only, against an earlier plan revision than the one specifying universal toggles), the `default`-masking gap, the two mock `editable_at` mismatches, the 7 stale domain-map keys, two stale committed artifacts (`docs/settings_liveness.json`, `docs/settings_field_census.json`/`.md` — regenerated via their own `--write` scripts after `settings.py`/`api/pilots_api.py` line numbers shifted), and this bullet's own wrong tunable-field count (was mis-stated as 157). **A ninth bug, found only by an actual live browser check after the audit's own fixes landed** (not by any of the 6 audit agents, none of which ran a real browser session against the fixed code): `webapp/src/api/mock.ts`'s `mockLiveness()` had no entry for `OPTIONS_EARNINGS_CRUSH_ENABLED` in its `MOCK_DEMO_ONLY_STATES` override map, so it fell through to the generic live_safe classification and rendered an interactive Toggle for a field that provably does nothing — reproduced live at `/settings/reference`, fixed by adding the override, and regression-tested (`SettingsReference.test.tsx`'s new mock-parity test calling the REAL `mockApi.getSettingsReference()` rather than a hand-rolled fixture). **That regression test's own first draft was itself broken and silently passed against the unfixed bug** — this describe block's shared `beforeEach` spies `api.getSettingsReference`, and since `client.ts` exports `api = USE_MOCK ? mockApi : liveApi` as the SAME object reference (not a wrapper), that spy clobbers `mockApi.getSettingsReference` for every test in the file; the fix needed an explicit `vi.restoreAllMocks()` inside the test body before calling the real mock, verified by manually reverting the bug and confirming the test failed, then restoring it and confirming it passed. Tests: `tests/test_settings_reference.py` (25 tests, incl. `TestReferenceWriteIndexDerivation`, `TestSettingsReferenceWrite`, `TestSettingsDomainsHygiene`), `tests/test_pilots_api_tunables.py` (incl. per-group field-membership tests for the two new groups), `tests/test_settings_keysets.py`, `tests/test_feature_flags_registry.py`, `webapp/src/screens/SettingsReference.test.tsx` (8 tests, incl. Toggle rendering, ordinary-write, dangerous-confirm, cancel, and the real-mock-module no_op regression check). **Step 4f update (2026-09):** the options-desk, circuit-breaker and ETF settings named above (`OPTIONS_EARNINGS_CRUSH_ENABLED`, `CIRCUIT_BREAKER_ENABLED`, `MULTI_BROKER_GATEWAY_ENABLED`, the "Options Desk Automation" group) were retired and the empty "ETF Transmission" domain dropped (13 domains). No boolean `no_op` field remains, so the mock's real no_op example is `PROMPT_MAX_CHARS` and the backend no_op-exclusion tests inject a fake liveness artifact; `_TUNABLE_INDEX` is 92 and `_FMP_INDEX` 30.

- **Cache Long/Short advisory strategy (`engine/cache_long_short_engine.py`, `data/cache_long_short_store.py`, 2026-08)**: a tax-loss-harvesting overlay — advisory only in V1, no broker order is ever submitted regardless of settings. Single-operator schema (no `User_ID`/`Portfolios` — matches `transactions_store.py`'s convention, not a multi-tenant design): `CacheLongShortPosition`/`CacheLongShortTaxLot`/`SecurityProxy` tables via `data/cache_long_short_store.py`'s `CacheLongShortStore` (SQLAlchemy declarative idiom). Real math, not mocked: `calculate_beta` reuses `processing_engine.calculate_rolling_beta` over `HistoricalStore.get_bars`; `find_correlated_proxy`/`check_correlation_drift` reuse `pairs_ondemand.analyze_pair` via `data.market_data.get_provider()` (the same lightweight `CompositeProvider` `POST /data/pairs/analyze` uses — NOT `DataEngine`, which lacks the `get_intraday_bars` method `pairs_ondemand` needs); `check_wash_sale` is a real SQL query against closed tax lots in the last 30 days. Split across the two existing FastAPI services rather than a new `APIRouter` (no such pattern exists elsewhere in this codebase): `POST /data/cache-long-short/simulate` (interactive, on-demand — mirrors `POST /data/pairs/analyze`'s exact pattern) lives in `api/data_api.py` because `api/pilots_api.py` is AST-guarded against importing `processing_engine`, even transitively-in-intent (see `pilots-endpoint` skill); the read/write endpoints (`GET/POST /pilots/cache-long-short/*`) live in `api/pilots_api.py` and read/write only through `pilots/cache_long_short.py` (a dependency-light helper, stdlib+settings+the store only), never the heavy engine. `settings.CACHE_LONG_SHORT_ENABLED` (default `False`) gates the `main_orchestrator.py` background TLH/correlation-drift scanner; `settings.CACHE_LONG_SHORT_WRITES_ENABLED` (default `False`) gates `POST /pilots/cache-long-short/{start,approve-bulk}` alongside the command token, per the pilots-endpoint skill's "own risk class, dedicated flag" rule — both default `False` because this changes what a trading strategy recommends, exactly the carve-out the bullet above describes. `CACHE_LONG_SHORT_WRITES_ENABLED` is a non-secret `ALLOWED_KEY` as of 2026-08-08 (see the GUI Command Center env-write safety bullet above) and is exposed as a toggle on the Cache Long/Short Strategy settings screen (`webapp/src/screens/SettingsCacheLongShort.tsx`, `GET/PUT /settings/cache-long-short`) — this changes only who can see/flip the boolean via a settings editor gated by `GENERAL_SETTINGS_WRITES_ENABLED` plus a typed confirmation (it's a `settings_keysets.DANGEROUS_KEYS` member), not what the flag itself gates: the `/pilots/cache-long-short/{start,approve-bulk}` endpoints still separately require `CACHE_LONG_SHORT_WRITES_ENABLED=True` at request time regardless of how it got set.

- **FMP-based paper trading engine (`execution/fmp_paper_broker.py`, `data/paper_account_store.py`, 2026-08)**: `FMPPaperBroker(BrokerBase)` — a drop-in broker adapter (gets `OrderManager`'s kill-switch + `PreTradeRiskGate` protection for free, no separate wiring) that fills orders against real FMP quotes adjusted by `execution.cost_model.TieredCostModel`'s realistic commission/slippage/spread, instead of Alpaca's paper sandbox. `data/paper_account_store.py`'s `PaperAccountStore` tracks virtual cash/positions/orders in SQLite across restarts. Selected via `settings.BROKER_BACKEND` (`"fmp_paper"` default | `"alpaca"`) in `main_orchestrator.py`'s broker construction — now defaults to `"fmp_paper"`. A runtime force-fallback safety guard ensures fallback to paper if live trading is requested without valid Alpaca credentials, verified by a preflight check. `settings.PAPER_BROKER_WRITES_ENABLED` gates all write operations to the paper store, which is managed via the new Paper Broker webapp UI surface. `settings.FMP_PAPER_STARTING_CASH` (default `100000.0`) seeds a fresh paper account. **Multi-Leg Option Paper Trading (2026-08)**: Supports both equity and full multi-leg options trading (e.g. Bull Call Spreads, Bear Put Spreads, Iron Condors, Straddles, Strangles, Calendar Spreads). Multi-leg orders execute atomically via `PaperAccountStore.apply_multi_leg_fill` with honest bid/ask spread pricing (net debit/credit calculation), $0.65/contract/leg commission modeling, short position / sell-to-open tracking (`qty < 0`), and dynamic mark-to-market option valuation via Black-Scholes using underlying spot quotes. Market orders instant-fill; LIMIT orders fill if already marketable against the net quote (otherwise rejected cleanly). **Quick Trade / any-symbol access + auto-execute symbol override (webapp, 2026-08)**: the paper-order path was already unrestricted end-to-end (`FMPPaperBroker`, `PaperAccountStore`, and the `/brokerage/options/order` endpoint never checked a symbol against `WATCHLIST`/`watchlist.txt`/held positions) but the Paper Broker screen exposed no way to reach an untracked symbol directly. `webapp/src/screens/PaperBroker.tsx` now has a "Quick Trade — Any Symbol" panel (a free-text `SymbolInput` → `GET /data/quotes` for a live quote → an inline `EquityOrderTicket` (`webapp/src/components/EquityOrderTicket.tsx`, which posts to `POST /pilots/paper-broker/order` → `pilots/paper_equity_order.py`; split out of the options ticket in 2026-09 so equity paper trading has no options-desk dependency), plus a "View Options Chain" link into the existing `/symbol/:ticker/options` route for options on that same untracked ticker) so any FMP-quotable symbol can be paper-traded without first adding it to the watchlist/pipeline universe; a failed/missing quote shows an honest error rather than opening a ticket with a fabricated `$0` spot price. The "⚡ Automated Strategy Options Execution" auto-scan also gained an optional operator-supplied symbol-list field — blank preserves the exact existing default (`getStrategyOptionsCandidates`/`executeStrategyOptions` called with no `symbols` arg, which `pilots/paper_broker.py`/`execution/options_paper_executor.py` resolve to `settings.WATCHLIST`), populated it scans exactly those symbols instead, independent of the pipeline's tracked universe — both API methods already accepted a `symbols?: string[]` argument that the UI simply never surfaced. A full sector/industry/strategy symbol *screener* (browse-by-attribute, not just trade-by-ticker) was explicitly scoped OUT of this change and remains a follow-up: no FMP symbol-search-by-name or screener endpoint exists anywhere in this codebase yet (`data/fmp_client.py` only wraps per-symbol lookups), and tier availability for one is unverified. **Symbol search & sector/industry screener (webapp, 2026-08, closing the item above)**: `data/fmp_client.py::search_name`/`search_symbol`/`company_screener`/`available_sectors`/`available_industries` wrap FMP's real `/search-name`, `/search-symbol`, `/search-company-screener`, `/available-sectors`, `/available-industries` endpoints — live-verified 2026-08 via an external FMP MCP connector (a separate, real FMP account; NOT yet exercised through this repo's own client or the operator's own `FMP_API_KEY`/tier, see `docs/FMP_INTEGRATION.md` §9 for the honest verification-status statement and the manual eyeball-gate before trusting it live). `data/fmp_screener.py` (a new standalone module, deliberately NOT added to `CompositeProvider`/`FMPProvider` — mirrors the `peers()`/`fetch_peer_group` precedent: a universe-browse capability has no per-symbol fallback shape) exposes `search_symbols`/`screen_companies`/`list_sectors`/`list_industries`, gated by a single `settings.FMP_SCREENER_ENABLED` (default `True`), never raises (degrades to `[]`). Three new `api/data_api.py` endpoints (`GET /data/symbol-search`, `GET /data/screener`, `GET /data/screener/filters`) follow `GET /data/peers/{symbol}`'s exact shape (flag check → honest `{"reason": ...}` short-circuit, never a 500). New webapp screen `webapp/src/screens/SymbolScreener.tsx` (nav section `"research"`, plus a Marketplace "Explore" tile for mobile reachability) offers free-text search, a sector/industry/market-cap/price/beta/dividend filter form, and a few pure client-side filter presets ("Large Cap Tech", "Dividend Income", "Low Vol" — no new quant scoring introduced). Selected symbols hand off via URL query params — this codebase's one existing cross-screen pattern (`Commands.tsx`'s `?builder=`) — into Paper Broker's existing Quick Trade panel (`?quickTradeSymbol=`) or auto-execute scan input (`?scanSymbols=`), both read via a mount-once `useSearchParams()` effect. New env var: `FMP_SCREENER_ENABLED`.

- **Spot data download + honest "add to watchlist" flow (`api/data_api.py::trigger_symbol_backfill`, `webapp/src/components/options/OptionsOrderTicket.tsx`, 2026-08)**: Phase 1 of the Symbol Screener universe-integration scoping. New `POST /data/backfill/{symbol}` (`require_write_token`-gated, matching `PUT /data/universe`'s fail-closed posture) forces a full `settings.BARS_BACKFILL_DAYS` bar backfill for one arbitrary symbol into a write-mode `HistoricalStore()` — every REST-facing symbol GET in this file deliberately uses `readonly=True`, whose live-fetch-but-never-persist short-circuit is exactly why a symbol discovered via the Symbol Screener or Quick Trade never actually landed in local storage before this endpoint existed; ports `investyo_mcp_server.py::trigger_data_engine`'s write-mode MCP-tool body into a REST route the webapp can call directly. Returns `{"symbol", "rows_persisted", "last_bar_date", "status": "ok"|"no_data"}`, never a fabricated success (CONSTRAINT #4). The existing "+ Add to Watchlist" button (`OptionsOrderTicket.tsx`, and since 2026-09 its equity-only sibling `EquityOrderTicket.tsx` → `POST /agentic/watch`) was previously `console.error`-only on failure — a `409 watchlist_env_precedence` or a missing `FOLLOW_API_TOKEN` produced zero on-screen feedback. It now surfaces failures visibly, and a successful add additionally fires the new backfill endpoint (non-blocking — a backfill hiccup never rolls back the watchlist-add confirmation, CONSTRAINT #6), showing a secondary "Backfilled N bars of price history" note. `webapp/src/api/client.ts::watchCandidate` was also fixed to extract `POST /agentic/watch`'s structured `{"error": tag, "message": ...}` error detail — the shared `http()` helper's generic `String(body.detail)` would have stringified that object literally (`"[object Object]"`), defeating the point of surfacing an honest server error. A successful fill on a symbol NOT in the tracked universe (checked via the existing `webapp/src/components/universeCache.ts` cache) now shows an inline "isn't in your tracked universe — add it?" prompt (Add / Not now) instead of auto-clearing after 2 seconds — an explicit user action, never a silent auto-add.

- **Widened symbol lookup — FMP-backed dropdown everywhere (`webapp/src/components/SymbolInput.tsx`, 2026-08)**: Phase 2 of the Symbol Screener universe-integration scoping. The shared `SymbolInput` combobox (9 call sites — Data Explorer, Signal Breakdown, Forecast Viewer, Sentiment Dynamics, Sector Selection, Pairs Radar ×2, Cache Long/Short's `ConfiguratorWizard`, Paper Broker's Quick Trade, Universe Manager) previously suggested only from the tracked universe (`GET /universe`). Now also debounce-queries `GET /data/symbol-search` (FMP's full symbol universe) and renders untracked matches under a "Not yet tracked" section, with any already-tracked duplicate excluded. New `enableFmpSuggestions` prop (default `true`) lets a screen opt out — `SectorSelection.tsx` does, since `GET /sector/selection` only reads persisted DB state and an untracked symbol there is a guaranteed honest-empty dead end. New `trackedSymbols` prop lets a caller override the shared `universeCache` fetch with its own list — fixes a real pre-existing bug in `UniverseManager.tsx`: this screen's entire purpose is editing `DEFAULT_TICKERS` (`GET/PUT /data/universe`), but its `SymbolInput` was suggesting from a DIFFERENT list, the pipeline-snapshot `GET /universe`. `UniverseManager.tsx::addSymbol` also now fires `POST /data/backfill/{symbol}` (Phase 1's spot-data-download endpoint) non-blockingly after a successful add. Both new props default to today's exact prior behavior at the 7 call sites that don't pass them.

- **FMP-primary-source audit (2026-08-21)**: an operator-requested audit confirming FMP is genuinely the primary source (not just in `settings.py` docstrings) across data-layer routing, webapp charts, LLM/MCP grounding, and test coverage. Findings and fixes: (1) a CONSTRAINT #4 fabrication bug in `pilots/volatility_surface.py::get_volatility_surface_data()` (a hardcoded `$500`/`$150` spot-price fallback on quote failure — see that module's own bullet above for the fix and regression test); (2) five `investyo_mcp_server.py` MCP tools (`get_ticker_context`, `run_backtest`, `plot_equity_curve`, `plot_portfolio_equity`, `get_portfolio_summary`) that called `yfinance` directly, bypassing `MARKET_DATA_PROVIDER="fmp"` — now routed through `get_provider()` (see `docs/architecture/observability-and-apis.md`'s dedicated bullet); (3) a real test-coverage gap — no test previously exercised `CompositeProvider()` against genuinely untouched `Settings()` defaults, only hand-patched ones — closed by `tests/test_market_data.py::TestCompositeProviderGenuineDefaultRouting`; (4) confirmed, on evidence (not assumption), that `SENTIMENT_SOURCES`'s exclusion of `fmp_news` from its default is correct, not a gap — it would double-fetch the same `stock_news` endpoint `NewsCatalystSignal.pre_compute()` already calls FMP-first every cycle, the identical reasoning that already excludes `finnhub` — and documented that reasoning explicitly in `settings.py`'s field description (previously unexplained). One disclosed, still-open risk this audit could NOT close: `scripts/verify_fmp_bars.py` — the hard gate this file already calls mandatory before trusting `FMP_BARS_ADJUSTMENT` — has never been run against a live account (no live-market network access in this sandbox), despite `FMP_BARS_ENABLED=True` being the live default; see that setting's own field description in `settings.py` for the honest standing caveat.

- **Options Tab (2026-08)**: Dedicated Options Chain explorer and interactive Strategy Builder in the Pilots PWA. Powered by `OptionsDataProvider` ABC — `CompositeOptionsProvider` (`data/market_data.py`) itself only wraps `YFinanceOptionsProvider` for the raw chain (strikes, IV, bid/ask); the live spot-price injection from FMP happens one layer up, in the `GET /data/options/chain/{symbol}` handler (`api/data_api.py`), which separately calls `data.market_data.get_provider().get_latest_quote()` and honestly returns HTTP 503 rather than fabricating a price if that quote fails (CONSTRAINT #4) — for accurate Black-Scholes Greeks calculations. The UI features a "Builder" mode that auto-targets optimal strikes by delta (e.g., 16 delta strangles, vertical spreads, calendar spreads) using a `findClosestStrikeByDelta` utility, and visualizes the strategy with `OptionsPayoffChart.tsx`. The order ticket includes a Live/Paper execution toggle, defaulting to Paper mode. Only advisory features are supported (no live order routing).

- **Options Portfolio Risk & Aggregate Greeks Engine (`pilots/options_risk.py`, 2026-08)**: Computes position-level and portfolio-wide net Greeks ($\Delta_{\text{net}}$, $\Delta_{\$}$, $\Gamma_{\text{net}}$, $\Theta_{\text{daily}}$, $\mathcal{V}_{\text{1\%}}$, and $\beta$-weighted $\Delta_{\text{SPY}}$) across equity and multi-leg option positions. Re-derives spot quotes and live IV per leg; excludes unresolvable positions from aggregate sums (never zero-filled) and surfaces them via `positions_with_missing_data` and UI warning banners. Enforces a `< 1e-12` degenerate-denominator guard on $\sigma\sqrt{T}$, an explicit 0DTE fallback to intrinsic delta ($\Delta = \pm 1.0$ ITM, $0.0$ OTM; $\Gamma = \Theta = \mathcal{V} = 0$), and a settings-driven risk-free rate (`settings.OPTIONS_RISK_FREE_RATE`, default 0.045). Adheres to the AST import boundary by strictly excluding `processing_engine` imports from `pilots/options_risk.py`. Supported by interactive backtesting via `validation/options_harness.py`, Stage 4 ML Meta-Labeling for trade gating and sizing multipliers $\in [0.30, 1.50]$ via `ml/options_meta_labeler.py`, and automatic expiration cash settlements via `PaperAccountStore.settle_expired_options`. Surfaced via `GET /pilots/paper-broker/greeks`, `/pilots/options/backtest`, `/pilots/options/meta-model/*`, `/pilots/paper-broker/settle-expired`, and the Pilots PWA Paper Broker screen.

- **Options Dispersion & Implied Correlation Arbitrage Engine (`pilots/dispersion_trading.py`, 2026-08)**: Decomposes index variance and weighted constituent variances to derive institutional implied correlation ($\bar{\rho}_{\text{implied}}$) via the Driessen, Maenhout, Vilkov (2009) model. Computes historical weighted realized correlation ($\bar{\rho}_{\text{realized}}$) to evaluate correlation spreads ($\Delta \rho = \bar{\rho}_{\text{implied}} - \bar{\rho}_{\text{realized}}$) and regime opportunities (Long Dispersion vs Short Dispersion). Calibrates vega-neutral constituent contract allocations ($\sum \mathcal{V}_{\text{constituent}} \approx \mathcal{V}_{\text{index}}$) and constructs multi-leg `DispersionBasket` orders. Executes multi-ticker straddles atomically into `PaperAccountStore` under `strategy_name="Dispersion Arbitrage"`.

- **Dynamic options lifecycle, delta hedging, vol surface & scenario matrix (2026-08)**: archived to `legacy/` with the options desk (2026-09, step 4b); its settings were retired in step 4f. Full history in git and `docs/known_issues/`. The paper-account items below (numbered as before) stay live:
  7. **CONSTRAINT #6 and #4 fix (2026-08-22, Paper Execution)**: `data/paper_account_store.py`'s atomic order processing functions (`apply_multi_leg_fill`, `apply_roll_fill`) silently defaulted to a zero fill price (`float(leg.get("fill_price", 0.0))`) if a leg was missing one, and did not validate if a `fill_price` was genuinely positive. This allowed corrupt options positions to be tracked in the paper portfolio. Fixed by adding a rigid `fill_price > 0.0` validation check that raises `ValueError` (rejecting the entire atomic order - Constraint #6). `pilots/dispersion_trading.py`'s client-supplied basket dicts were also updated to explicitly validate positive `fill_price` and strike consistency against real spot data before constructing a `DispersionBasket`. Hardcoded spot price fabrications were removed in `pilots/vol_mispricing.py`. A purge script `scripts/purge_corrupt_paper_options.py` was introduced to scan the SQLite DB for any previously opened options with a non-positive `avg_entry_price`, delete them, and reverse any residual cash impact.
  8. **PR 872 remediation (2026-08-24) — real closed-trade ledger, migration safety, attribution safety, and a live-DB test-contamination incident, all in one 6-agent pass**: PR 872's original scope (a `paper_closed_trades` table so a flattened/expired position's realized PnL, entry time, and holding period survive instead of being deleted with the position row — see `docs/architecture/execution.md`'s `data/paper_account_store.py` entry for the full detail) shipped with five real arithmetic/safety bugs, all found and fixed in this remediation pass: (1) **option PnL was inflated 100x** — `_record_closed_trade` re-applied the options ×100 contract multiplier on top of prices every writer in this codebase already stores as a per-contract dollar amount; fixed by removing the double-multiply, and `settle_expired_options` now converts its per-share intrinsic value to per-contract before handing it to `_record_closed_trade` so the ledger row agrees with the real cash settlement by construction. (2) `realized_pnl_pct` now reports `None` — never a fabricated `0.0` (CONSTRAINT #4) — when `avg_entry_price` is degenerate (`< 1e-12`), rather than blowing up or lying. (3) A new `PaperPosition.entry_ts` column (set on open/flip-through-zero, left untouched while averaging in) makes `holding_period_days` a real measurement instead of always `None`. (4) **`paper_positions` migration atomicity**: rebuilding the legacy single-column-PK schema into the current composite `(symbol, strategy_id)` PK schema used to run inside a broad `except Exception: logger.error(...)` that could commit a partial RENAME→CREATE→INSERT→DROP sequence (Python's stdlib `sqlite3` driver implicitly commits before DDL under plain `engine.begin()`, verified directly) — fixed via a raw `sqlite3` connection in explicit manual-transaction mode plus a pre-migration whole-file backup; a migration that can't complete cleanly now raises and construction fails (CONSTRAINT #6) instead of silently starting against a partially-migrated table. (5) **`allow_untagged_fallback` (Bug 7) made strict opt-in, default `False`**: `apply_fill`/`apply_multi_leg_fill`/`apply_roll_fill` used to unconditionally auto-borrow an opposite-signed position from the legacy `'untagged'` strategy bucket, silently misattributing PnL on a strategy's first order against a symbol with legacy inventory; the new `retag_position()` primitive is the explicit, deliberate replacement for moving a legacy position onto its real `strategy_id` by hand. Separately, the `transactions_store` bridge added by this same PR (gated by `settings.PAPER_TRADES_BRIDGE_TO_TRANSACTIONS_ENABLED`, default `False`) was fixed to reuse the caller's own session via a SAVEPOINT (`session.begin_nested()`) instead of opening a second connection mid-transaction, closing both a wrong-database bug (a bare `TransactionsStore()` re-resolving the default URL regardless of this store's own `db_url`) and a measured ~30-100x same-process WAL-writer contention cost; it fails OPEN, not closed, on a bridge write failure (the paper-side close still commits; only the bridge write is lost, logged at WARNING and counted). **A live-DB test-contamination incident occurred and was fixed during this same remediation, stated plainly rather than euphemized**: the new bridge's implicit write path had no test-isolation guard, and the real, shared `~/.stockpy_local/quant_platform.db`'s `trades` table was found holding 260 rows of synthetic test data (confirmed 100% contamination — the table had 0 rows before the bridge feature existed, every row bridge-tagged) with timestamps spanning roughly a day, predating the discovering session. Fixed via a new autouse `conftest.py` fixture, `_isolate_paper_and_transactions_db_in_tests`, routing both `PaperAccountStore`'s and `TransactionsStore`'s default DB resolution to an isolated per-test temp-file DB (a plain `:memory:` and a shared-cache memory URI were both tried and rejected first — see the incident doc for why). The live DB was backed up via `sqlite3 ... ".backup"` and the cleanup DELETE itself was run by the human operator, not the agent — blocked for the agent by the sandbox's destructive-write safety gate. See `docs/known_issues/pr872_live_db_test_contamination_2026.md` for the full incident write-up and the three regression tests (`tests/test_paper_account_store.py`'s `*_isolated_from_live_db`/`*_share_the_same_isolated_db` tests) that prove the fixture's contract.
  9. **Closed Trades read surface + strategy attribution UI (2026-08-24)**: item 1's `paper_closed_trades` table had a write path but no read path or UI — `data/paper_account_store.py::get_full_closed_trades(symbol=None, limit=100)` (mirroring `get_full_orders`'s readonly-safe cold-start guard and most-recent-first/`limit` shape; never coerces `realized_pnl_pct` from `None` to `0.0` — CONSTRAINT #4) is exposed via `pilots/paper_broker.py::get_closed_trades()` and a new fail-open-read-tier `GET /pilots/paper-broker/closed-trades`, rendered as a new Closed Trades table on the webapp Paper Broker screen. Fixed a real bug found along the way: `pilots/paper_broker.py::get_positions()` silently dropped `strategy_id`/`pilot_id`/`experiment_arm` from its response even though `PositionSnapshot` already carried them — both the Positions and Orders tables now render a Strategy column (Orders' backend response already included these fields; only the webapp type/table were missing). See `docs/architecture/execution.md`'s `data/paper_account_store.py` entry and `docs/architecture/webapp-and-gui.md`'s `PaperBroker.tsx` entry for full detail.
  10. **Retrospective Learning Loop & Trade Autopsy Engine (2026-09)**: closes the gap left by items 1/9 above — a closed trade's realized PnL/holding period was recorded, but nothing recorded WHY it was opened (a real signal, or a manual click) or how the hold period actually played out against price. `data/paper_account_store.py`'s own `paper_entry_snapshots` table is a deliberately **forward-only** capture of decision provenance/conviction/macro-regime/factors — a snapshot is written ONLY at the moment a paper position is newly opened (`_create_entry_snapshot`, wired into `apply_fill`/`apply_multi_leg_fill`/`apply_roll_fill`), never backfilled or inferred after the fact. `pilots/retrospective_composer.py::compose_trade_retrospective(s)` joins a `paper_closed_trades` row with (a) a real MFE/MAE/Edge-Ratio recompute over the hold window via `evaluation_engine.evaluate_portfolio()` (injected an isolated single-trade `TransactionsStore` via its `transactions_store=` DI parameter, not a process-global monkeypatch) and (b) the entry snapshot lookup — **`decision_context_status` is NEVER inferred from `strategy_id`/`pilot_id` after the fact**, only from whether a real snapshot row exists. `pilots/retrospective_narrative.py::build_trade_narrative` renders a 3-clause plain-text narrative from a composed record — strictly template-based, **zero LLM calls** — with an explicit missing-data variant for every field that can be `None`/NaN, and free-text fields sanitized before interpolation. `pilots/retrospective_insights.py::generate_batch_retrospective_insights()` reuses `evaluation_engine.calibration_curve()` verbatim for the reliability diagram and separately buckets closed trades into automated/manual/unrecorded cohorts — the cohorts are STRUCTURALLY separate keys with **no combined/"overall" figure anywhere** in the response. `PaperAccountStore.get_bridge_completeness_metrics()` measures the paper→`transactions_store` bridge's completeness; `completeness_pct` is `None` only when nothing was genuinely checkable, and a real, measured `0.0` (bridge never ran) is reported as such, never suppressed. All three `pilots/retrospective_*.py` modules never raise (CONSTRAINT #6) and are dependency-light-exempt (heavy imports are lazy, inside function bodies) per `tests/test_pilots_strategy_matrix.py`. Three new fail-open-read-tier endpoints on `api/pilots_api.py`: `GET /pilots/paper-broker/trades/{trade_id}/retrospective`, `GET /pilots/paper-broker/retrospective/insights` (`limit`/`symbol`/`strategy_id`), `GET /pilots/paper-broker/bridge/metrics`. New webapp screen `webapp/src/screens/RetrospectiveJournal.tsx` (`section: "trading"`, a Marketplace Explore tile, `TabGuide tabKey="retrospective"`) plus `RetrospectiveDetailModal.tsx`: a Trade Journal tab of composed+narrated entries (an honest "evaluation data unavailable" state when excursion data isn't available, distinct decision-provenance badges, null-aware rendering throughout) and a Pattern Insights tab with isolated cohort cards. **Disclosed, not yet closed**: the generic options auto-scan path and three automated options paper-trade writers — `pilots/dispersion_trading.py`, `pilots/copula_stat_arb.py`, `pilots/zero_dte_engine.py` — don't yet pass a `pilot_id` to `apply_multi_leg_fill` (confirmed by grep), so a Dispersion Arbitrage/Copula Stat Arb/0DTE Momentum Breakout trade reports `decision_context_status="not_captured"` even though it genuinely was signal-driven; `execution/options_paper_executor.py`'s automated strategy-options auto-scan (`provenance="automated:options_auto_scan"`) and `pilots/paper_broker_options_order.py` (Quick Trade / order ticket, `provenance="manual"`) are wired today. See the dedicated "Retrospective Learning Loop & Trade Autopsy Engine" bullet elsewhere in this file for the full milestone-by-milestone detail, and `docs/architecture/simulation-eval-reporting.md`'s `evaluation_engine.py`/`transactions_store.py` entry for this feature's place in the broader evaluation subsystem.

- **Historical persistence (`data/historical_store.py`, Tier 2.3)**: `HistoricalStore` is the single SQLite write-path for OHLCV bars (Phase 1), account snapshots (Phase 2), fundamentals (Phase 3), and FRED macro series (Phase 3). `fetch_account_snapshot()` MUST follow the three-tier read order — DB first → JSON cache → live — so the DB row survives a network outage. `get_fundamentals()` caches typed columns AND full `raw_json` from the provider; missing fields → `NaN`, NEVER `0.0` (CONSTRAINT #4). `get_macro()` stores both VIXCLS and T10Y2Y from `DataEngine.fetch_macro_history()` and serves them as named pd.Series; fresh rows (age < `MACRO_REFRESH_HOURS`) skip the network call. Import `HistoricalStore` lazily (inside function bodies, not at module top) everywhere — `data/robinhood_portfolio.py`, `processing_engine.py`, and `macro_engine.py` all use lazy imports to avoid circular imports. `HISTORICAL_STORE_ENABLED=False` disables all DB routing without touching the JSON-cache or live-fetch tiers. **Phase 3 settings**: `FUNDAMENTALS_REFRESH_DAYS` (int, default 1 — days before a cached fundamentals row is re-fetched), `MACRO_REFRESH_HOURS` (int, default 12 — hours before FRED macro series are topped up). **Existing settings**: `HISTORICAL_STORE_ENABLED` (bool, default `True`), `BARS_BACKFILL_DAYS` (int, default 504). **Cache-poisoning guard (2026-07)**: `get_fundamentals()` never upserts a totally empty provider response — doing so would read back as a "fresh" all-NaN cache hit on the next call and suppress retry until `FUNDAMENTALS_REFRESH_DAYS` elapses; a non-empty response with some missing fields is still cached as-is (that's legitimate partial data, not a poisoned cache). **`schema_version` (2026-07)**: `_ensure_tables()` stamps/checks a single-row `schema_version` table (`CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` in `data/historical_store.py`) on every construction. Additive migrations (`ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`) remain the actual upgrade mechanism and need no version bump; the stamp exists to WARN (never raise — CONSTRAINT #6) when a DB file was written by a newer build than the one currently reading it, the one drift additive migration can't self-detect.

- **Robinhood auto-refresh gate (`settings.ROBINHOOD_AUTO_REFRESH_ENABLED`, default `False` as of 2026-08 — was `True`)**: `fetch_account_snapshot()`'s Tier 3 (live login) runs automatically whenever the cached snapshot exceeds `max_age_hours` (default 20h) — every one of its ~8 call sites (GUI panels, `investyo_mcp_server.py`, `api/data_api.py`, `data/portfolio_sync.py`, `engine/llm_commentary.py`) inherits this with no shared cooldown, so once credentials start failing, every poll from every surface independently re-attempts a Robinhood login. **Default flipped to `False`** alongside the device-approval login rewrite below: an unattended Tier-3 attempt can never succeed once login requires a human tapping approve on their phone, so leaving automatic re-attempts on by default just spawns a doomed login-worker subprocess every cycle once the cache goes stale. `False` makes the live fetch strictly opt-in: Tier 3 is skipped whenever `force=False`, returning the best available cached snapshot (DB latest, else JSON cache) regardless of staleness, and raising only when nothing is cached anywhere. Only `force=True` (i.e. `python3 main.py --refresh-account`, or the webapp's Connect/Refresh flows, both of which run the login in the supervised worker described below) ever logs in. New env var: `ROBINHOOD_AUTO_REFRESH_ENABLED`.

- **Robinhood closed-trade ingest — the operator's real sells were invisible everywhere (2026-08, `data/broker_fills_store.py`, `data/robinhood_orders.py`, `data/robinhood_login_worker.py`, `pilots/trade_history.py`)**: root cause, confirmed by reading the code and a real operator account's local state: `data/robinhood_orders.py`'s FIFO reconstruction engine existed and was tested, but (a) its only production caller, `pilots/realized.py`, deliberately injects an empty `orders_fetcher` so a web request never triggers a login — cache-only by design — and nothing else ever called it with a real one; (b) even a real call would have crashed on a dead `from data.robinhood_portfolio import _login` import left over from the device-approval rewrite above. Net effect: the `trades` table stayed at 0 rows regardless of how many real trades the operator closed, and a sold-to-zero symbol silently vanished from the analysis universe (`main.py`'s `held ∪ WATCHLIST ∪ watchlist.txt ∪ discovered`) with no "sold" state — just gone. Fixed in three parts. **(1) Ingest repair**: `data/robinhood_orders.py:fetch_filled_orders(orders_fetcher=None)` now dispatches on `os.environ["RH_LOGIN_WORKER"] == "1"` (uses `r.get_all_stock_orders()` directly inside the worker) or raises the existing `RobinhoodApprovalRequired` outside it (caught by the existing degrade-to-cache path — zero behavior change for the two callers that already inject their own fetcher). `data/robinhood_login_worker.py`'s `refresh`-mode branch now also calls `_ingest_orders_best_effort(emit)` immediately after the account snapshot fetch, using the SAME authenticated session — one device-approval push produces both artifacts instead of a second push per refresh. Four non-negotiable properties enforce this can never turn a successful snapshot refresh into a reported failure: its own nested try/except, strictly-after-the-snapshot ordering, `refresh`-mode only (never `connect`), and a `settings.RH_ORDER_INGEST_BUDGET_SECONDS` (default 60s) wall-clock bound well inside the 180s login deadline, backed by `settings.RH_ORDER_SYMBOL_RESOLVE_MAX` (default 200) capping the per-instrument `get_symbol_by_url` network fanout via a durable resolver-cache seed/write-back (`data/broker_fills_store.py`'s `BrokerInstrumentSymbol` table) so only the FIRST ingest for a given instrument pays that cost. **(2) Durable store**: `data/broker_fills_store.py` (own `Base`/table/`session_scope`/`readonly=True` convention, matching `validation/validation_history_store.py`) persists **fills** (keyed by Robinhood's own `order_id`), not reconstructed `ClosedTrade`s — a dedup key derived from a `ClosedTrade` would be unstable, since FIFO pairs a sell with whichever buy lot is still in the fetch window, and that pairing shifts once an older buy ages out on a later fetch; `closed_trades()` simply re-runs the pure, unmodified `reconstruct_closed_trades()` over the full persisted fill history on every read. `record_fills()` is idempotent (insert-the-diff by `order_id`; a divergent re-fetch keeps the latest value and logs a WARNING; an empty `order_id` is skipped and counted, never given a fabricated key). Gated by `settings.BROKER_TRADE_INGEST_ENABLED` (default `True` — this flag IS the fix, a deliberate exception to "new settings default to today's behavior"). Requires the same session-wide autouse `conftest.py` DB-isolation fixture as `ValidationHistoryStore`/`ExecutionAuditStore` (`_isolate_broker_fills_db_in_tests`) since `recently_closed_symbols()` (below) is reachable from `main._build_universe`/`data.portfolio_sync.build_sync_report`, both exercised by dozens of pre-existing test files with no store of their own. **Sizing isolation is structural, not conventional**: this module never imports `transactions_store`, and nothing under `sizing/`/`execution/` imports it — enforced by an AST guard in `tests/test_broker_fills_store.py` — because production sizing (`strategy_engine.py`'s aggregate Kelly path at `strategy_engine.py:765`, `engine/advisory.py`'s own aggregate path) reads `TransactionsStore.closed_trades_df()` unfiltered by strategy; writing the operator's manual, discretionary Robinhood trades into that table would silently move live position sizing on every symbol the moment the closed-trade count crossed `sizing/kelly.py`'s `MIN_TRADES_REQUIRED` cold-start threshold — confirmed as a real, not hypothetical, risk: with the `trades` table empty, `_raw_kelly_or_vol_target_sizing` returns weight `0.0` for every symbol TODAY (`scale_in = min(1.0, 0/30) = 0.0`); the operator's real 17-round-trip history would have moved `scale_in` to `0.567` and silently turned position sizing ON platform-wide in a single commit had it landed in `trades`. **(3) Universe retention**: `settings.CLOSED_POSITION_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 180, a deliberate behavior change; `0` restores the pre-2026-08 universe exactly) + `settings.CLOSED_POSITION_RETENTION_MAX_SYMBOLS` (default 25) keep a fully-sold symbol in the analysis universe for a bounded window after its most recent real SELL fill (never a buy-only symbol — retention keys off the last sell only), via `data.broker_fills_store.recently_closed_symbols()`. `main.py::_build_universe` unions this 4th (really 6th, counting discovery/fallback) source LAST — after both the `SYMBOL_RATING_AUTO_DROP_ENABLED` subtraction and the `DEFAULT_TICKERS` empty-fallback decision — because a retained symbol has `held=False` and would otherwise be immediately re-subtracted by auto-drop, or (if unioned even earlier) would silently suppress the empty-universe fallback on an otherwise-cold account. `data/portfolio_sync.py::build_sync_report`/`resolve_universe` carry the identical logic for the webapp/sync path via a synthetic `"closed:recent"` watchlist (`CLOSED_RECENT_LIST_KEY`); `resolve_universe`'s own auto-drop protected set needed an explicit widen to include it (unlike `main.py`, its `tracked` set is built BEFORE the auto-drop subtraction, so the same trap reappears there). A genuine bug found and fixed in the same pass: `async_sync_now()`'s `DEFAULT_TICKERS` persist previously would have baked every retained symbol into `.env` permanently (`DEFAULT_TICKERS` never expires and `resolve_universe` unions it in unconditionally) — now explicitly excludes the synthetic list's symbols from what gets persisted. **New Trade History screen**: `pilots/trade_history.py` (`GET /portfolio/trade-history`, fail-open `require_read_token`) reads the durable store with real pagination and a per-symbol filter — distinct from the pre-existing `pilots/realized.py`/`GET /portfolio/realized`, which stays cache-only and capped at 100 trades for the Portfolio screen's summary panel; `summary` is computed over the full filtered history, not just the returned page. `webapp/src/screens/TradeHistory.tsx` (nav section `"research"`, a Marketplace "Explore" tile, and a "See all →" link from Portfolio's Realized performance panel). **Opt-in analytics wiring (NOT sizing)**: `settings.EVAL_BROKER_TRADES_ENABLED` (default `False`, preserving today's exact NaN behavior) lets `evaluation_engine.evaluate_portfolio()` fall back to broker-reconstructed closed trades for MAE/MFE/'Edge Ratio' on a symbol with NO internal `transactions_store` history — internal history always wins when present, and this never writes `transactions_store`'s `trades` table or reaches any sizing path; `calibration_curve` needs no separate gate since a broker trade genuinely carries no platform-issued `conviction` to report, and its existing `dropna(subset=["conviction", ...])` already excludes these rows. `get_all_stock_orders` (the underlying Robinhood API) is equities-only and short-sale excess is dropped, never fabricated — see `docs/known_issues/robinhood_order_history_window_and_fifo_limits.md` for the full disclosed-limits writeup. New env vars: `BROKER_TRADE_INGEST_ENABLED`, `RH_ORDER_INGEST_BUDGET_SECONDS`, `RH_ORDER_SYMBOL_RESOLVE_MAX`, `CLOSED_POSITION_RETENTION_DAYS`, `CLOSED_POSITION_RETENTION_MAX_SYMBOLS`, `EVAL_BROKER_TRADES_ENABLED`. Tests: `tests/test_broker_fills_store.py`, `tests/test_universe_retention.py`, `tests/test_pilots_trade_history.py`, `tests/test_evaluation_broker_trades_fallback.py`, `tests/test_robinhood_login_worker_orders_ingest.py`, plus extended coverage in `tests/test_robinhood_orders.py`, `tests/test_pilots_api.py`, `tests/test_pilots_strategy_matrix.py`.

- **Portfolio & Watchlist Synchronization (`data/portfolio_sync.py`, Task 1.4)**: any code that needs "the complete universe the operator is tracking" — beyond the held-only or watchlist-only slice — MUST go through `build_sync_report()` / `async_sync_now()`, never re-derive the union ad-hoc. Coverage status drives downstream eligibility: pricing-dependent metrics must consume `CoverageStatus.FULL` symbols only (or `QUOTES_ONLY` if fundamentals aren't required); `EQUITY_ONLY` symbols stay in the equity / holdings view but are excluded from quote-driven calculations (no fabricated price proxy — CONSTRAINT #4). The Sync engine never raises on a coverage gap (dead-letter resilience); it logs an INFO diagnostic per affected symbol and records the failure mode on `SymbolStatus.diagnostic`. **New env var:** `SYNC_WATCHLIST_FILES` (colon-separated paths to additional plain-text watchlist files, one ticker per line, `#` = comment; missing files tolerated silently). The GUI Live Inventory tab's "🔄 Sync Now" persists the discovered universe to `DEFAULT_TICKERS` in `.env` via `shared/env_io.py` (`DEFAULT_TICKERS` is already in `ALLOWED_KEYS` from the Settings Manager tab).

- `config.py`'s `COLUMN_SCHEMA` now includes `RSI_2` and `SMA_5` (both `"format": "number"`/`"currency"`), feeding `processing_engine.calculate_technical_metrics()`'s `ta.rsi(length=2)` / `ta.sma(length=5)` columns (added alongside the existing RSI(14)/SMA(50)/SMA(200) calculations — same causal, vectorized pattern, verified lookahead-free).

- `StrategyEngine.evaluate_security()` and both orchestrator call sites (`main.py`, `main_orchestrator.py`) now also accept/pass `rsi_2: float` and `sma_5: Optional[float]`, propagated into the per-ticker `row` Series as `RSI_2`/`SMA_5`/`Close` for signal modules that need them.

- Position sizing ("Kelly Target") is no longer derived from `score`/`sortino_ratio`/`edge_ratio` brackets. The single source of truth is `StrategyEngine._calculate_kelly_sizing(realized_vol)` → `sizing.kelly`/`sizing.vol_target`, fed by `settings.KELLY_FRACTION` (0.5), `settings.KELLY_CAP` (0.20), `settings.VOL_TARGET` (0.10), `settings.MAX_LEVERAGE` (2.0), `settings.MAX_POSITION_WEIGHT` (1.0). The vol-target fallback (insufficient trade history) can internally compute up to `MAX_LEVERAGE` (2.0x), but `_calculate_kelly_sizing` always applies a final `min(weight, settings.MAX_POSITION_WEIGHT)` clamp before returning — chosen as the middle ground between the old score-bracket system's 25% ceiling and an uncapped 2.0x. Neither `sizing.kelly` nor `sizing.vol_target` enforce this themselves; the clamp lives in `StrategyEngine`, so any other caller of those modules directly must apply its own ceiling.

- **Sizing-decision pipeline + portfolio cap + guardrail telemetry (`sizing/position_sizer.py`)**: `StrategyEngine.evaluate_security()` composes the HMM regime multiplier and meta-label composite on top of `_calculate_kelly_sizing`'s already-clamped weight via ONE ordered function, `size_position()`, instead of the previous inline arithmetic — and reports `SizingDecision.was_capped`/`.binding_constraint` (`"kelly_cap"`/`"vol_target_leverage"`/`"max_position_weight"`/`"portfolio_gross"`/`"escalation"`/`None`), telemetry that did not exist before. The routine HMM regime multiplier is deliberately EXCLUDED from this flag (it's expected signal-driven derating, not a guardrail cap — `Regime_Multiplier` stays its own separate field) so the guardrail doesn't fire on every risk-off cycle. New **portfolio-level gross exposure cap** (`apply_portfolio_gross_cap`, `settings.MAX_PORTFOLIO_GROSS` default `2.0`) is applied cycle-wide by `pipeline/production_steps.py::StrategyEvalStep` on top of — not instead of — the per-name `MAX_POSITION_WEIGHT` ceiling; a capped name's telemetry is overridden to `"portfolio_gross"`. `"Sizing_Was_Capped"`/`"Sizing_Binding_Constraint"` are new schema-driven `config.COLUMN_SCHEMA` columns (Sheet + `state_snapshot.json` on both writers, parity enforced by `tests/test_state_snapshot_parity.py`) plus new GUI surfaces (Strategy Matrix regime-impact section; Observability's "Sizing Cap-Event Audit Trail"). The advisory path (`engine/advisory.py`) keeps its own deliberately-tighter, decoupled cap (`CONFIG["max_single_position_pct"]`=5%) and gets its OWN guardrail telemetry (`Recommendation.sizing_was_capped`/`.sizing_binding_constraint`) rather than being routed through `size_position()`. New opt-in **cap-aware escalation** (`settings.SIZING_CAP_ESCALATION_ENABLED`, default `False`) down-weights a name capped for `SIZING_CAP_ESCALATION_THRESHOLD_CYCLES` (default 5) consecutive cycles by `SIZING_CAP_ESCALATION_FACTOR` (default 0.5), reading history from the new durable `sizing/cap_audit_store.py` (`sizing_cap_events` table, `RunHistoryStore`-pattern DB store, gated by `settings.SIZING_CAP_AUDIT_ENABLED` default `True`), which also drives an OPT-IN `observability.alerts.send_alert("WARNING", ...)` threshold alert (`settings.SIZING_CAP_ALERT_ENABLED` default `False` — KELLY_CAP binding is routine for an established Kelly book, so this doesn't surprise existing deployments with new WARNING logs on upgrade; `settings.SIZING_CAP_ALERT_THRESHOLD_PCT` default 30%). All seven new settings default to today's exact behavior (audit-only aside, which is additive/observational) — see `docs/architecture/signal-engines.md`'s `sizing/position_sizer.py` bullet for the full ordered pipeline, the byte-identical per-symbol equivalence proof, the portfolio-cap sensitivity sweep, and the fixed dead-lettered-ticker telemetry fabrication bug (this repo's sandboxed dev/CI environments have no live-market network access, so a `validation/harness.py` PBO/DSR/Sharpe/MaxDD re-verification could not be run as part of this change — see that doc and the introducing PR for the honest bottom line before enabling non-default settings live).

- `processing_engine.calculate_fundamental_metrics()` now accepts an optional `realized_vol_60d_map: dict[str, float]` parameter (sourced from `calculate_technical_metrics()`'s `Realized_Vol_60D`, itself exposed from `calculate_momentum_metrics()`'s already-lookahead-free internal computation) and computes five multifactor raw inputs per ticker: `book_to_market` (1/P/B), `earnings_yield` (1/P/E), `quality_factor_score` (mean of whichever are present among `returnOnEquity`, `operatingMargins`, `grossMargins` — all fractions; mean not sum keeps 1/2/3-metric tickers on one cross-sectional z-score scale, identical `Quality_Z` to the old `ROE + operating margin` sum when all tickers carry the same two metrics; falls back to `-debt_to_equity` only when none of the three are present), `low_vol_score` (negative 60-day realized vol), `log_market_cap`. All five are `NaN` (never fabricated) when the underlying field is unavailable. `calculate_technical_metrics()` also now surfaces `Realized_Vol_60D` itself (`NaN`, not `0.0`, when <60 valid daily returns exist).

- `config.COLUMN_SCHEMA` now includes `Value_Z`, `Quality_Z`, `LowVol_Z`, `Size_Z`, `Multifactor_Composite` (all `"format": "number"`), produced by `signals/multifactor.py`'s `pre_compute()` and written back into `dashboard_df` by `main_orchestrator.py` immediately after `global_registry.run_pre_compute()` (the only signal module whose *outputs*, not just its inputs, need writing back to the dashboard — XSec momentum writes its inputs before `pre_compute` and never needs an outputs write-back since its rank dict isn't surfaced as its own columns the same way).

- `config.COLUMN_SCHEMA` now also includes `sellRange` (`"format": "string"`, header `"Sell Range"`), placed immediately after `buyRange` in the TACTICAL EXECUTION section. Produced by `strategy_engine.apply_sell_side_range` (see strategy_engine.py entry above) and written into `dashboard_df` by both `main.py` and `main_orchestrator.py` from `strategy_output['sellRange']`. Also surfaced in (a) the `main_orchestrator.py` JSON payload printed at end of run, (b) `_write_state_snapshot()`'s per-signal dict as `"buy_range"` / `"sell_range"` for the Streamlit observability dashboard, (c) the `daily_report_template.html` summary table column and per-ticker advice card. (The Google Sheet sink that also wrote it was archived in step 4e, and `config.get_headers()`/`get_rename_mapping()` were removed in step 4f.)

- `config.COLUMN_SCHEMA` now also includes `HMM_Risk_On_Probability` (`"format": "percent"`). `run_pipeline()` in `main_orchestrator.py` now accepts an optional `data_engine: IDataProvider` parameter, used to call `MacroEngine.compute_hmm_risk_on_probability(tech_raw.get('SPY'))` and pass the result into `MacroEconomicDTO(hmm_risk_on_probability=...)`. `processing_engine.process_macro_regime()` surfaces it into the dict consumed by `compile_dashboard()`, which writes `HMM_Risk_On_Probability` into every row (`NaN`, never fabricated, when the HMM didn't run). `main()`'s offline-fallback branch (constructing a fresh `MockDataEngine`) now reassigns the outer `de` reference too, so `data_engine=de` passed to `run_pipeline()` is always consistent with whatever engine actually produced `tech_raw`/`macro_raw`/`fund_raw` for that run.

- **Gaussian HMM Regime Detector tuning & diagnostics (`regime/hmm_regime.py`, `validation/regime_diagnostics.py`, `scripts/audit_regime_model.py`, 2026-08)**: `HMMRegimeDetector` supports operator-tunable hidden state counts (`settings.HMM_N_STATES`, default 3), covariance structures (`settings.HMM_COVARIANCE_TYPE` `"diag"` default | `"full"` | `"spherical"` | `"tied"`, with multi-dimensional trace variance sorting for `full`), EM iterations (`settings.HMM_N_ITER`, default 150), and tolerances (`settings.HMM_TOL`, default 1e-4). `build_feature_matrix` supports optional credit spread (`BAMLH0A0HYM2`, `settings.HMM_CREDIT_SPREAD_FEATURE_ENABLED` default `False`) and 20D-60D vol term structure spread (`settings.HMM_VOL_TERM_SPREAD_FEATURE_ENABLED` default `False`). `MacroEconomicDTO` dynamically sources its downgrade threshold (`settings.HMM_RISK_ON_DOWNGRADE_THRESHOLD`, default 0.30) and recession agreement threshold (`settings.HMM_RISK_OFF_AGREEMENT_THRESHOLD`, default 0.70) from `settings`. `validation/regime_diagnostics.py` delivers causal walk-forward backtesting and model comparison (AIC/BIC), executable via `python -m scripts.audit_regime_model [--compare] [--json]`. New settings: `HMM_COVARIANCE_TYPE`, `HMM_N_ITER`, `HMM_TOL`, `HMM_RISK_ON_DOWNGRADE_THRESHOLD`, `HMM_RISK_OFF_AGREEMENT_THRESHOLD`, `HMM_CREDIT_SPREAD_FEATURE_ENABLED`, `HMM_VOL_TERM_SPREAD_FEATURE_ENABLED`, `KILLSWITCH_VIX_THRESHOLD_AGREED`, `KILLSWITCH_SAHM_THRESHOLD_AGREED`. Tests: `tests/test_hmm_no_lookahead.py`, `tests/test_hmm_synthetic.py`, `tests/test_hmm_state_persistence.py`, `tests/test_macro_hmm_integration.py`, `tests/test_regime_diagnostics.py`. **State-labeling fix (2026-08-22)**: `identify_states_by_vol()` previously mislabeled hidden states for `covariance_type="spherical"` (hmmlearn's public `covars_` getter returns a malformed shape for spherical, silently tripping an unlogged arbitrary-index fallback on every fit) and could mislabel `"tied"` (ranked states by an undirected mean-vector norm instead of a directional risk proxy) — reproduced a genuine crash regime labeled `"bull"`, which could have silently under-reported risk to `MacroEconomicDTO.killSwitch`'s HMM-agreement fast-trigger. Both are fixed (`spherical` now reads the compact internal `_covars_` array, matching this file's own warm-start precedent; `tied` now ranks by a directional risk-feature mean); the length-mismatch fallback now logs an error instead of failing silently; `n_states >= 4` now correctly labels the worst state `"bear"` instead of a generic `"state_<n-1>"`. `tied` covariance, while now correctly *labeled*, has a separate structural limitation for regime detection specifically (verified: its EM fit collapses to a single dominant state on realistic calm-vs-turbulent variance ratios) and is not recommended — see `docs/known_issues/hmm_regime_state_mislabeling_spherical_tied.md` and `docs/regime_model_tuning_guide.md`'s Covariance Structures section for full detail.

- **Advisory state-snapshot telemetry parity (`reporting/state_snapshot.py`)**: the advisory entry-point writer (`write_state_snapshot`, used by the `main.py` advisory path) now emits the SAME telemetry the `main_orchestrator.py` `_write_state_snapshot()` path already surfaced, so switching to the advisory orchestrator no longer blanks the GUI Observability / Report-Viewer tabs. Top-level macro recession telemetry (`sahm_rule`, `high_yield_oas`, `yield_curve`, `hmm_risk_on_probability`) is sourced from the injected `macro_dto`; each per-signal entry carries `garch_vol` and `hmm_risk_on` plus the five multifactor keys (`value_z`, `quality_z`, `lowvol_z`, `size_z`, `multifactor_composite`). `garch_vol` reads from `engine.advisory` `Recommendation.key_indicators`. The five multifactor keys are likewise sourced from `Recommendation.key_indicators` — `engine.advisory.evaluate()` looks up `context_extras["multifactor_scores"][symbol]` (the same dict `main._build_context_extras()` builds via `global_registry.run_pre_compute()` and threads into `StrategyEngine.evaluate_security()` for scoring) and copies `Value_Z`/`Quality_Z`/`LowVol_Z`/`Size_Z`/`Multifactor_Composite` onto the snake_case `key_indicators` keys the snapshot writer reads. `NaN` (JSON `null`, never a fabricated 0.0 — CONSTRAINT #4) when `context_extras` is `None`, pre-compute failed, or the symbol has no entry (e.g. microcap-excluded, or not part of this cycle's universe). Covered by `tests/test_advisory.py::TestContextExtrasThreading` (engine-level: real values populate, absence degrades to NaN) and `tests/test_state_snapshot_advisory.py` (writer-level serialization contract).

- **Options matrix integrity (`technical_options_engine.py` + Gravity STEP 38)**: every premium-selling directive rendered by the GUI's Technical Options Matrix tab, or returned by `OptionsPricingRecommender.generate_strategy_pricing_matrix`, MUST satisfy two structural invariants — **strike grid** (every leg on the `$0.50` USD grid via `_on_strike_grid`) and **delta-target tolerance** (resolved BS delta within `±0.05` of the conventional target in `EXPECTED_DELTA_TARGETS`). The validator is `validate_directive_integrity(directive)`. Gravity `step_38_options_matrix_integrity_audit` exercises schema hydration, strike-grid compliance, delta tolerance, off-grid rejection, VIX>30 regime gate, CREDIT EVENT regime gate, and low-IVR debit-spread routing. Tests: `tests/test_options_matrix.py`.

- **Market-data layer (`data/market_data.py`)**: All quote/bar/fundamentals fetches outside the existing `DataEngine.fetch_technical_raw()` path MUST go through `CompositeProvider` (the `MarketDataProvider` ABC). `CompositeProvider` auto-selects Alpaca (when `ALPACA_API_KEY` + `ALPACA_SECRET_KEY` present) or yfinance (zero config). `MARKET_DATA_PROVIDER` env-var can force either. Fundamentals are Yahoo statement-derived via `YahooFundamentalsProvider` (`data/yahoo_fundamentals.py`, `SOURCE="yahoo_computed"`, PRIMARY) with a raw yfinance `.info` (`YFinanceProvider`) emergency fallback; `FUNDAMENTALS_SOURCE` (`"yahoo"` default | `"yfinance_info"`) selects the primary. Finnhub is NO LONGER a fundamentals source (`FinnhubProvider` retained but unwired — `FINNHUB_API_KEY` now feeds only `signals/news_catalyst.py`). Two scale rules: `dividendYield` emitted as a fraction (consumed as-is), `debtToEquity` emitted ×100. Beta = `Cov(stock,SPY)/Var(SPY)` over `BETA_LOOKBACK_DAYS` (504). In-process quote cache (TTL `MARKET_DATA_QUOTE_TTL_SECONDS`=30 s, never persisted to disk). `is_stale=True` on any yfinance quote — treat as delayed by design. `MarketDataError` is the typed exception for dead-letter resilience. `get_provider()` / `reset_provider()` manage the module-level singleton. **New settings**: `settings.MARKET_DATA_PROVIDER`, `settings.FUNDAMENTALS_SOURCE`, `settings.BETA_LOOKBACK_DAYS`, `settings.FINNHUB_API_KEY` (news-only), `settings.MARKET_DATA_QUOTE_TTL_SECONDS`.

- **Broker execution is best-effort**: errors in `_execute_broker_orders` are logged as ERROR and never propagate to crash the analysis pipeline. The pipeline's value (signals, HTML report, JSON payload) must not be held hostage to broker connectivity.

- **Broker abstraction**: all order submission in orchestrator and strategy code MUST go through `OrderManager` (never directly to `AlpacaBroker` or any concrete broker). Type-annotate against `BrokerBase`; swap paper ↔ live by changing one constructor call.

- **Idempotency**: every order intent submitted via `OrderManager.submit_order_with_idempotency` gets a deterministic `client_order_id` from `make_client_order_id(strategy_id, symbol, side, qty, timestamp)`. Do NOT fabricate or reuse IDs manually.

- **Dry-run is enforced at manager level**: `OrderManager._submit_with_retry` checks `intent.dry_run` before calling any broker — this means MockBroker tests in the test suite also correctly see the dry-run guard without implementing it themselves. AlpacaBroker also has its own redundant guard as a belt-and-suspenders, but the manager-level check is the authoritative one.

- **Reconciliation alerts**: set `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` in `.env` to a Slack/Discord incoming-webhook URL; `reconcile_state` fires it on any position drift. The webhook call uses `urllib.request` (no extra dependency). Failures in the alert path are logged but never swallowed silently in a bare except.

- **New settings for broker**: `DRY_RUN` (default `false`), `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` (default `None`). Both are optional; the orchestrator degrades gracefully when either is absent.

- **Free-first news/attention sourcing expansion (2026-07)**: four follow-on sources/features closing the gaps between the existing free-first sentiment pipeline (`data/sentiment_sources.py`, `signals/news_catalyst.py`) and local FinBERT actually running. (1) **Local FinBERT made real**: CPU `torch` moved to `requirements-optional.txt` (was previously undeclared, so FinBERT silently ran in keyword-lexicon fallback everywhere); `signals/news_catalyst.py::score_headlines()` is a new batched entry point (`settings.FINBERT_BATCH_SIZE`, default 16) returning the full 3-class softmax per headline instead of one collapsed scalar, backed by a content-hash `finbert_score_cache` table in `data/historical_store.py` (`settings.FINBERT_SCORE_CACHE_ENABLED`, default `True`) so unchanged headlines aren't rescored; `_score_headline()` stays a thin, cache-bypassing wrapper for existing callers. (2) **`GoogleNewsRSSSource`** (`"google_news"` in `data/sentiment_sources.py`) — Google News RSS search, `f"{symbol} stock"` query (no company-name-resolution dependency added), fuzzy-title dedup (opaque redirect links defeat URL dedup), `settings.GOOGLE_NEWS_LOOKBACK_WINDOW` (default `"7d"`). (3) **EDGAR full-text search (EFTS)** — `EdgarSource` gained an opt-in extension ingesting 10-K/10-Q filing BODY text (not just the always-on 8-K headline feed), gated by `settings.EDGAR_FULLTEXT_ENABLED` (default `False`), `settings.EDGAR_FULLTEXT_FORMS` (default `"8-K,10-K,10-Q"`), chunked to `settings.EDGAR_FULLTEXT_CHUNK_TOKENS` (default 512); shares `data/edgar_fundamentals.py`'s SEC throttle. (4) **`Sector_Heat_Factor`** (GDELT per-sector article-VOLUME/attention proxy, Gaussian-smoothed, `data/sentiment_sources.py::compute_sector_heat_factors`) and **`Attention_Score`** (Wikipedia Pageviews-based investor attention, DEG(2011)-style transform, `data/attention_sources.py`, optional 429-tolerant `pytrends` fallback that is never load-bearing) both ship as `pipeline/production_steps.py`-populated dashboard feature COLUMNS only — no `signals/` module, no `SIGNAL_WEIGHTS` entry — gated by `settings.SECTOR_HEAT_ENABLED`/`settings.WIKIPEDIA_ATTENTION_ENABLED`/`settings.PYTRENDS_ENABLED` (all default `False`), and are `ORCHESTRATOR_ONLY_FIELDS` in `tests/test_state_snapshot_parity.py` (no advisory-path source exists for either). **All ten new settings default to today's exact behavior** — nothing above is active until explicitly enabled. See `docs/architecture/data-layer.md`'s `data/sentiment_sources.py`/`data/attention_sources.py` entries and `docs/signals/sector_heat_factor.md` for full detail.

- **Google Trends Live Fetching & Persistence (`data/google_trends_client.py`, `data/trends_store.py`, 2026-08)**: Live SVI data fetching is enabled via `pytrends` with robust process-wide rate limiting, exponential backoff, and a cooldown circuit breaker that mirrors the `_gdelt_get` design, preventing HTTP 429 errors. Raw downloaded windows and stitched continuous SVI series are persisted in a local SQLite database (`raw_trends_downloads` and `stitched_google_trends` tables in `trends_store.py`) enabling point-in-time reads and minimizing redundant network calls.

- **Phase 4: LSTM-Attention Forecaster (`cnn_lstm_worker.py`, `ml/asvi_feature_engineering.py`, 2026-08)**: Deep LSTM-Attention model trained on a 15-feature sliding window (Target ASVI, Sector-Proxy ASVI, Open, High, Low, Close, Volume, RSI_14, EMA_12, EMA_26, MACD, MACD_Signal, MACD_Hist, Realized_Vol_60D, SMA_50). The attention mechanism exposes raw `attention_weights` for temporal lag visualization. Sector ASVI utilizes the real `FMPDataLoader.get_fundamentals()` dynamically (inheriting the native 6h `FUNDAMENTALS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` cache) mapping the string sector to a SPDR ETF proxy (e.g. Technology -> XLK) via `SECTOR_TO_ETF`, securely falling back to SPY on miss. Neural execution safely leverages the `cnn_lstm_worker.py` TF-isolated subprocess pool to avoid deadlocks. **Correction (2026-08-31 audit fix)**: this was false as shipped — `forecasting_engine.py::run_lstm_attention_forecast` called a nonexistent `cnn_lstm_process_pool.dispatch_to_worker` function, so every real dispatch raised `ImportError`, silently swallowed by the method's own broad `except Exception`, and returned a fake-looking `{"prediction": nan, ...}` sentinel with no visible error — the subprocess pool was never actually reached. Fixed to call the real `run_in_subprocess(func, args, timeout_seconds=, max_workers=)`, mirroring the pre-existing CNN-LSTM dispatch path exactly; end-to-end verified live in this environment (TensorFlow installed) — a real subprocess launched, trained, and returned a genuine non-NaN prediction. Diagnostics are wired through `POST /pilots/ml/lstm-attention-forecast`.

- **Shared GDELT rate limiter (`data/sentiment_sources.py::_gdelt_get`, 2026-07)**: both GDELT consumers — `GDELTSource` (`mode=artlist`, per-symbol tone documents) and `GDELTVolumeSource` (`mode=timelinevol`, the `Sector_Heat_Factor` path) — MUST issue every request through the single module-level limiter, never `requests.get` directly. The courtesy budget is per-HOST, so two independently unthrottled clients in one process is exactly how a 429 storm starts (same rule that made the now-archived `legacy/data/etf_holdings.py` reuse `data/edgar_fundamentals.py`'s `_throttle` instead of opening a second SEC client). The concrete failure: `GDELTSource.fetch()`'s windowed backfill is ONE request for a live per-cycle `since` and so looked healthy, but `scripts/backfill_sentiment_history.py --months 6` is ~26 unspaced requests per symbol — measured 2026-07-29 against a 33-symbol universe, GDELT answered HTTP 429 to substantially all of them, archiving nothing. Three bounds: minimum issuance spacing (`GDELT_MIN_REQUEST_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, default 5.0, lock held across the sleep), retry with exponential backoff honouring `Retry-After` (`GDELT_MAX_RETRIES` 2, `GDELT_RETRY_BACKOFF_SECONDS` 5.0), and a cooldown circuit breaker that SKIPS GDELT calls outright for `GDELT_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` (300) after `GDELT_COOLDOWN_THRESHOLD` (3) consecutive FAILED requests — 429, 5xx, or transport error alike — the bound that keeps an unresponsive host from turning a long backfill into hours of certain-to-fail requests while EDGAR/Finnhub/Reddit starve for wall-clock budget. Counting transport errors is deliberate and was corrected during this change: the first cut counted only 429s, and a live re-test found GDELT had switched to read-timing-out, so a single 6-month symbol still burned 262 s for zero documents (26 windows × a 10 s timeout). From the caller's side "refusing us" and "not answering us" have identical cost and identical remedy; requiring CONSECUTIVE failures is what keeps one flaky socket from opening the cooldown. Retries, by contrast, stay 429/5xx-only — an immediate retry of a read timeout just times out again at full cost, and a 404 is a bad query, not an overloaded host. Measured after the fix: 262 s → 30.3 s for that same symbol. `GDELTSource.fetch()` treats `GDELTUnavailable` — and only that — as a signal to abandon the remaining windows; every other per-window failure keeps skip-and-continue. New `SentimentSource.deadline` seam: `CompositeSentimentSource` checks its budget BETWEEN sources, which cannot interrupt a 26-window loop already running, so it now assigns `source.deadline = self._cycle_deadline` and any source that loops internally must consult `deadline_exceeded()`. **Unlike most flags in this codebase these defaults are NOT a no-op**: a live cycle now covers fewer symbols' GDELT documents per pass than the unthrottled (largely 429-ing) version attempted to, and GDELT publishes no hard rate-limit number, so these values are a conservative choice rather than a documented contract. `GDELT_MIN_REQUEST_INTERVAL_SECONDS=0` + `GDELT_MAX_RETRIES=0` + `GDELT_COOLDOWN_THRESHOLD=0` reproduces the prior behavior exactly. **New env vars**: `GDELT_MIN_REQUEST_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `GDELT_MAX_RETRIES`, `GDELT_RETRY_BACKOFF_SECONDS`, `GDELT_COOLDOWN_THRESHOLD`, `GDELT_COOLDOWN_SECONDS`.

- **Credential reads MUST go through `settings.X`, never `os.environ` directly (`signals/news_catalyst.py::build_finnhub_client`, fixed 2026-07)**: this function read `os.environ.get("FINNHUB_API_KEY", "")`, the one outlier among this codebase's credential-gated sentiment sources — `RedditSource`/`EdgarSource` in `data/sentiment_sources.py` both correctly read `settings.REDDIT_CLIENT_ID`/`settings.EDGAR_USER_AGENT`. Pydantic-settings' `env_file=".env"` loads `.env` into the `Settings` model directly; it does NOT also copy the value into the process's real `os.environ`. An operator whose only source for a key is `.env` (the documented, normal case) got a silently `None` client forever, indistinguishable from "this source genuinely has nothing this cycle" — no error, no warning. Confirmed live 2026-07-29: a real Finnhub key in `.env` produced zero documents across a 33-symbol, 6-month backfill until fixed. Existing tests masked this because they `patch.dict(os.environ, {"FINNHUB_API_KEY": ...})`, which DOES mutate real `os.environ` — unlike a `.env` file — so they never observed the gap; corrected to `patch("settings.settings.FINNHUB_API_KEY", ...)` matching the Reddit/EDGAR test convention. Any new credential-gated source MUST follow the `settings.X` pattern, never read `os.environ` for a value that might only live in `.env`. **Two further instances of the same bug class, fixed 2026-07-31**: `prompt_registry/registry.py::_build_registry_from_settings` and `prompt_registry/__main__.py::cmd_publish` read 8 keys via `os.environ.get(...)` instead of `settings.settings.X` (`PROMPT_REGISTRY_ENABLED`, `PROMPT_REGISTRY_PINS`, `PROMPT_REGISTRY_SIGNING_KEY`, `PROMPT_REGISTRY_BACKEND`, `PROMPT_REGISTRY_URL`, `PROMPT_REGISTRY_TOKEN`, `PROMPT_CACHE_DIR`, `PROMPT_CACHE_KEEP_VERSIONS`; `PROMPT_REGISTRY_PINS` is now read as a native dict, the redundant `json.loads` removed — `PROMPT_REGISTRY_CREDENTIALS` intentionally still reads `os.environ`, since no `settings.py` field exists for it yet); and `data/market_data.py`'s `CompositeProvider` (`__init__`, `_select_quote_provider`, `get_intraday_bars`, `get_fundamentals`, `YahooFundamentalsProvider._beta_period`) read `MARKET_DATA_PROVIDER`, `ALPACA_API_KEY`/`ALPACA_SECRET_KEY`, `FUNDAMENTALS_SOURCE`, `MARKET_DATA_QUOTE_TTL_SECONDS`, `MARKET_DATA_BARS_TTL_SECONDS`, `FUNDAMENTALS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`, `FUNDAMENTALS_NEG_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`, and `BETA_LOOKBACK_DAYS` the same wrong way — both switched to `settings.settings.X`.

- **Prompt Registry settings-plumbing fixes (2026-07-31)**: beyond the `os.environ`→`settings.X` fix above (`prompt_registry/registry.py`/`prompt_registry/__main__.py::cmd_publish`), a second, independent bug in the same module: `prompt_registry/__main__.py`'s `cmd_pin`/`cmd_rollback` called `json.dumps(dict(...))` on the pins payload BEFORE passing it to `shared.env_io.write_setting("PROMPT_REGISTRY_PINS", ...)` — but `write_setting` already JSON-encodes `_JSON_KEYS` values internally, so `.env` ended up with a JSON string literal wrapping real JSON (double-encoded). `_build_registry_from_settings`'s `isinstance(pins, dict)` check then failed silently and every CLI-set pin was dropped without error. Fixed by passing the plain `dict`. **Rule**: any `_JSON_KEYS` value passed to `shared.env_io.write_setting`/`write_many` MUST be a plain Python object, never pre-`json.dumps`'d — `write_setting` owns the encoding.

- **Degenerate-std guard convention**: any ratio that divides by a computed standard deviation or drawdown must guard with `< 1e-12` (or `>= 1e-12` for the "valid, proceed" branch), never an exact `== 0` / `> 0` check — floating-point noise from a constant or near-constant series lands *near*, not *at*, zero. The original incident: a flat/degenerate returns series produced `std() ≈ 1e-16`, and an exact-equality guard let `mean / std` explode to roughly `-7.7e16` in the Pilots PWA's Regime Navigator. The `1e-12` threshold matches the original site, `legacy/risk/etf_transmission.py::_DEGENERATE_STD` (archived 2026-09), and has since been propagated to: `validation/metrics.py::sharpe_ratio` and its CPCV per-path Sortino guard; `validation/harness.py`'s full-sample Sortino guard and two Calmar guards (in-sample and OOS-gate branches); `evaluation_engine.py::calculate_equity_curve_metrics`'s Sharpe and Calmar guards; `simulation_engine.py::cost_sensitivity_curve`'s std guard; `processing_engine.py::calculate_technical_metrics`'s Sortino guard (feeds the live `Sortino_Ratio` column); `signals/multifactor.py::_zscore_winsorize`'s std guard (feeds the live `Multifactor_Composite` column); and `scripts/refresh_validations.py`'s two Sortino-adapter guards (`_build_sortino_drawdown_adapter`, `_build_signal_replay_adapter`). Any new ratio-over-std/drawdown computation MUST use this same threshold, not a fresh exact-equality check.
