- **Graduated-degrade convention for N-way blends**: an aggregate/blend of independent estimators (signal modules, ensemble models, ETF-wrapper composites) must never use an `any()`/`all()` all-or-nothing readiness gate that lets one immature/missing component silence the others' real signal — exclude the immature component and renormalize/proceed over survivors instead. Reserve true all-or-nothing gating for structurally coupled objects (a covariance matrix) or deliberate worst-case-dominates safety gates (a stress-scenario deployability check), and comment explicitly why when using it. Generalizes `signals/multifactor.py`'s `.mean(skipna=True)` quality score and `signals/aggregator.py`'s per-module `is_active_in_regime`/`DISABLED_SIGNAL_MODULES` skip. Fixed at: `forecasting/forecast_tracker.py::compute_skill_weights_from_stats` (+ its two callers in `pilots/observability.py`), `legacy/risk/etf_transmission.py::build_etf_return_composite` (archived 2026-09), `signals/registry.py::compute_all`/`compute_all_vectorized`. See `docs/known_issues/graduated_degrade_all_or_nothing_blends.md`.

- **CNN-LSTM purged internal train/val split (`forecasting_engine.py`, `cnn_lstm_worker.py`, 2026-07 quant-integrity fix)**: `make_direct_multistep_windows`'s stride-1 supervised windows overlap by `lookback-1` raw rows, so Keras's own `validation_split=0.2` (driving `EarlyStopping`) left the last training windows near-duplicates of the first validation windows, optimistically biasing `val_loss`-based epoch selection — a training-procedure leak distinct from (and in addition to) the train/inference scaler leakage `fit_scalers_on_train` already fixed. `ForecastingEngine.purged_train_val_split` (mirrored pure-numpy in `cnn_lstm_worker.py::_purged_train_val_split` for the subprocess-isolated path) purges the overlapping windows and passes an explicit `validation_data=(X_val, Y_val)` instead of `validation_split`. Applied unconditionally (no settings flag — this only affects which epoch's weights `EarlyStopping(restore_best_weights=True)` selects, not the causal correctness of the emitted forecast). See `docs/architecture/signal-engines.md`'s `forecasting_engine.py` entry. Tests: `tests/test_forecasting_lookahead.py::TestPurgedTrainValSplit`, `tests/test_cnn_lstm_worker.py`.

- **Monte Carlo drift correction (F1 fix, 2026-09)**: Removed the `(μ − 0.5σ²)` Itô drift term from `run_monte_carlo` in `forecasting_engine.py`. Because `μ` (expected log-return) already incorporates the `−0.5σ²` drag relative to simple returns, subtracting it again resulted in a systematic downward bias (double correction) that artificially depressed long-horizon Monte Carlo bands. This was corrected unconditionally (no settings flag), shifting the bands up by exactly the expected magnitude.

- **Forecasting rebuild F1: measure what we publish (2026-09, `.claude/forecasting_rebuild_implementation_plan.md`)**: measurement only; no published `Forecast_*`, skill weight or signal changes. (1) The published blend is recorded to `forecast_errors` as model `blend` (only for a real, non-`Is_Fallback` blend) next to `naive`, on the recordable copy, never in `model_forecasts`. (2) One row per symbol × model × horizon × US/Eastern trading day: new nullable `forecast_day` column + partial index; `ForecastTracker.record_forecasts` UPDATEs the still-pending row with that key and INSERTs only if none matched. Matured and pre-F1 (NULL-day) rows are never rewritten. (3) `compute_skill_weights_from_stats` drops `blend` (and the never-run BERT-LLA ablations) from the arithmetic and never lets a measurement-only name end the cold start; `naive` otherwise stays in the arithmetic, so weights are byte-identical to pre-F1 (verified on all 1,278 live (symbol, horizon) pairs). (4) `ForecastTracker.skill_vs_naive` / `scripts/forecast_skill_report.py`: per-model median |log error| vs naive on the same (symbol, ET day) pairs, % beating naive, direction hit rate, sign-test p; sub-$1 excluded. (5) `scripts/clean_forecast_ledger.py` is DRY-RUN by default; `--categories` defaults to `a,b` (TEST rows, the 2026-08-14 Monte Carlo ~$100 seed rows). Category `c` (pre-F1 intra-day duplicates) is opt-in and must wait for the F3 naive gate: it would send 1,194 of 1,223 warm pairs back to the cold start, and the script prints that warning from the measured numbers. The script also reports which pairs' live skill WEIGHTS move (deleting the (b) rows raises Monte Carlo's weight for 54 (symbol, horizon) pairs, largest shift 0.337). `--apply` backs up first, deletes only the selected categories, and is for the operator to run. (6) `pilots/forecast_skill.py` reads `FORECAST_SKILL_WINDOW_DAYS`/`MIN_OBS` so the UI shows the weights actually used. **Disclosed indirect effect**: because the live blend uses skill weighting (`FORECAST_SKILL_WEIGHTING_ENABLED=true` on the operator install), fewer rows per day from now on means future skill weights accumulate one observation per day instead of ~20, so a newly-tracked symbol stays in the equal-weight cold start for ~30 trading days rather than ~2. Tests: `tests/test_forecast_rebuild_f1.py`, `tests/test_forecasting_engine.py::TestF1BlendRecordingNeverLeaksIntoBlend`, `tests/test_pilots_forecast_skill.py::TestSkillWeightsUseLiveBlendWindow`.

- **MFE/MAE fabrication risk removed (`evaluation_engine.py`, `pipeline/production_steps.py`, 2026-07)**: the core MAE/MFE computation (`evaluation_engine.py`'s `evaluate_portfolio`/`calculate_edge_ratio`) was already genuine — a real closed trade's intra-trade OHLC path from `transactions_store.py`, not fabricated — but two spots fell short of that bar and are now fixed. (1) `pipeline/production_steps.py` used to compute an "edge ratio" over a fictional 15-bars-ago-to-today window (no real trade behind it) and append it to the user-facing "Strategy Explainer Notes" as if it were a genuine post-trade evaluation; removed (its value was always overwritten anyway by the real per-trade computation later in the same step). (2) `evaluate_portfolio()`'s degraded fallback — deriving MAE/MFE from a single day's `High`/`Low` against `Entry_Price` when no real trade history existed, and hardcoding `position_type='long'` regardless of the real side — was removed; MAE/MFE now stay `NaN` (CONSTRAINT #4) in that case, matching every other "no real trade history" path. Tests: `tests/test_evaluation_no_history.py`, `tests/test_production_steps_edge_ratio_notes.py`.

- **Strategy validation harness genuine-OOS gate (`validation/harness.py`, `validation/metrics.py`, `settings.VALIDATION_HARNESS_OOS_GATE_ENABLED` default `False`, 2026-07 quant-integrity fix)**: `ValidationReport.sharpe`/`.max_dd`/`.sortino`/`.calmar`/`.hit_rate`/`.avg_trade_pct`/`.turnover` — everything feeding the `deployable` gate's `Sharpe > 0.5` / `MaxDD < 30%` criteria besides PBO/DSR — were computed from `self.strategy_fn(X, y, X, y)`, an in-sample "test" set identical to the training set, while only PBO/DSR were genuinely out-of-sample (via `CombinatorialPurgedCV`); separately, CPCV's own PBO/DSR Sharpes were computed on gross (cost-free) returns even though the in-sample leg applied `_apply_cost_model`'s turnover-scaled cost — an inconsistent cost basis between the gate's two halves. `run_cpcv_evaluation()` gained an optional `cost_model_fn` parameter (cost-adjusts every CPCV path's returns before any Sharpe/PBO/DSR/drawdown statistic) plus four new genuinely-OOS aggregates (`mean_oos_max_dd`/`mean_oos_sortino`/`mean_oos_hit_rate`/`mean_oos_avg_trade_pct`/`mean_oos_turnover` — the mean of each metric computed independently per CPCV path, not one concatenated curve, since CPCV's combinatorial test-block reuse across paths would double/triple-count dates in a naive concatenation). When the settings flag is `True`, the harness wires its cost model into CPCV and replaces the seven in-sample numbers with these OOS aggregates; `equity_curve`/`benchmark_curve`/`macro_benchmark_curve` stay on the full-sample series either way (a single non-overlapping OOS equity curve needs the AFML CPCV backtest-path-recombination algorithm, not implemented here — a real, separate follow-up, not silently faked). Default `False` reproduces every currently-recorded `docs/VALIDATION_STRATEGY_FIX_LOG.md` PBO/DSR/Sharpe/MaxDD baseline exactly — this sandboxed dev/CI environment has no live-market network access to re-verify the registered `STRATEGY_REGISTRY` fleet against the corrected numbers. `n_trials` (already persisted per-strategy but never displayed) is now also surfaced next to DSR in the main HTML validation report and the GUI Validation Lab table. See `docs/architecture/validation-and-signals.md`'s `validation/harness.py`/`validation/metrics.py` entries and `docs/VALIDATION_STRATEGY_FIX_LOG.md`'s 2026-07-29 harness-level addendum. Tests: `tests/test_metrics_cpcv_oos_aggregates.py`, `tests/test_harness_oos_gate.py`.

- **Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) data layer (`data/fmp_client.py` + `data/fmp_fundamentals.py`/`data/fmp_macro.py`/`data/fmp_feeds_company.py`/`data/fmp_feeds_market.py` + `FMPProvider` in `data/market_data.py`, 2026-07 — full reference: `docs/FMP_INTEGRATION.md`)**: primary source for quotes/bars/fundamentals with `MARKET_DATA_PROVIDER="fmp"`, `FUNDAMENTALS_SOURCE="fmp"`, and all 14 `FMP_*_ENABLED` capability booleans + `FMP_QUOTES_REALTIME` defaulting to `True` (deliberate operator decision for active FMP deployment), with automatic fallback to the existing Alpaca/yfinance/Yahoo chain on any failure. `FMP_API_KEY` in `.env` is required for live network calls. Five diagnostic feeds (analyst consensus/grades, an earnings calendar + surprise history, a treasury-rate/econ-indicator macro supplement into the existing `macro_history` table, economics calendar events (`Next_Macro_Event`/`Next_Macro_Event_Date`), and insider-trading + dated sector snapshots) are diagnostic-only dashboard columns — **never a `SignalModule`, never a `SIGNAL_WEIGHTS` entry**, since none has point-in-time history on day one (FMP serves only the CURRENT analyst consensus, for example) and "a signal the repo cannot backtest cannot earn a weight" is also the no-lookahead guarantee mechanism here, not just policy. Pipeline execution in `pipeline/production_steps.py` shares a single `_fmp_deadline` wall-clock budget across all per-symbol loops. `FMP_FALLBACK_ENABLED` (default `True`) governs whether a failure falls through the chain or propagates. **The single highest-risk setting is `FMP_BARS_ADJUSTMENT`** (default `"dividend-adjusted"`): the incumbent yfinance path is `auto_adjust=True` (split AND dividend adjusted), while FMP's `light`/`full` EOD variants are split-only — `full` looks like the obvious choice and is wrong, and a mismatch corrupts every return series, indicator, GARCH fit, and backtest *plausibly* (nothing fails loudly). `scripts/verify_fmp_bars.py` (network-marked, not part of the pytest suite) is a hard gate — it must exist and PASS (max abs relative close diff `< 1e-4` on KO/JNJ/AAPL over 2 years) before `FMP_BARS_ADJUSTMENT` is changed from its default or before trusting raw bars. **Starter-tier limitation, permanent and not fixable by this integration**: `Institutional Velocity` stays hardcoded `0.0` — Form 13F is Ultimate-only and Starter has no short-interest feed either, so no FMP endpoint on this account can fill either half of that calculation; `data/etf_holdings.py`'s SEC N-PORT path (archived to `legacy/` 2026-09) is likewise unchanged (ETF/mutual-fund holdings are also Ultimate/Enterprise-only). See `docs/FMP_INTEGRATION.md` for the full 31-setting reference, the manual eyeball-gate checklist required before flipping any flag live, and the honest risk/verification-limitations list; see `docs/architecture/data-layer.md`'s "The FMP data layer" entry for the full module-by-module architecture.

- **FMP as the primary company-news provider (`data/fmp_client.py::stock_news`/`parse_news_published_date`, `data/sentiment_sources.py::FMPNewsSource`, `signals/news_catalyst.py::fetch_company_headlines`/`fetch_next_earnings_any`, `settings.FMP_NEWS_ENABLED` default `True` (flipped from False, 2026-08 PR #737), 2026-08 — full reference: `docs/FMP_INTEGRATION.md` §7)**: prompted by an operator hitting a `FINNHUB_API_KEY is not set ... (or finnhub-python is not installed)` error from `scripts/backfill_news_history.py` alongside a separate `.env`-resolution bug (see the `.env`/venv-bootstrap bullets below) — the decision was to make FMP PRIMARY for company news/earnings dates, with Finnhub kept as an opt-in fallback rather than removed. `FMP_NEWS_ENABLED` + `FMP_API_KEY` is the two-gate convention every other FMP feed here follows. Verified live 2026-08: `/news/stock` covers ≥6 months of real history (vs. Finnhub's free-tier ~3 month cap) with working `from`/`to`/`page`/`limit` pagination (bounded by `FMP_NEWS_MAX_PAGES` default `10`, `FMP_NEWS_PAGE_LIMIT` default `100`). Deliberately does **not** wrap `/news/press-releases` — that endpoint returned a plan-entitlement `"Restricted Endpoint"` rejection on the account this was verified with. **`publishedDate` is US Eastern Time, not UTC** — verified (not assumed) by cross-referencing a real GlobeNewswire article: FMP reported `"2026-08-02 14:51:00"` for a release whose own page states `"August 02, 2026 14:51 ET"`, an exact match; `parse_news_published_date` localizes via `ZoneInfo("America/New_York")` (DST-correct year-round) before converting to UTC — getting this wrong would have silently shifted every `news_history` day-bucket by up to 5 hours. Two new dispatcher functions in `signals/news_catalyst.py` — `fetch_company_headlines(symbol, lookback_days)` and `fetch_next_earnings_any(symbol)` — try FMP first and fall back to the existing, unchanged, still-exported `build_finnhub_client()`/`fetch_company_news()`/`fetch_next_earnings()` otherwise; `NewsCatalystSignal.pre_compute()` (internal method renamed `_score_via_provider`, `_score_via_finnhub` kept as an alias), `llm/research.py`'s Opal grounding packet, and `engine/agent_sentiment.py`'s Antigravity tool were all re-pointed at the two dispatchers, and each one's gate now accepts FMP-only configuration (previously required a Finnhub client). `data/sentiment_sources.py` gained a new opt-in `FMPNewsSource` (`name="fmp_news"`, registered in `_SOURCE_REGISTRY`/`_SOURCE_PRIORITY` ahead of `"finnhub"`, added to `signals/credibility.py`'s `_INSTITUTIONAL_SOURCES`) — an operator must add `"fmp_news"` to `SENTIMENT_SOURCES` for it to run; `FinnhubSentimentSource` is intentionally left untouched and Finnhub-specific, still independently selectable. **`"fmp_news"` is deliberately excluded from `SENTIMENT_SOURCES`'s default** (re-audited 2026-08-21, decision: leave as-is) for the identical reason `"finnhub"` already is — `NewsCatalystSignal.pre_compute()`'s `_score_via_provider()` → `fetch_company_headlines()` already calls the same `data.fmp_client.stock_news()` endpoint FMP-first, per symbol, every cycle whenever `FMP_NEWS_ENABLED`+`FMP_API_KEY` are set; adding `"fmp_news"` to the default would double-fetch that same endpoint per symbol per cycle rather than pull broader or different data. See `settings.py`'s `SENTIMENT_SOURCES` field description for the full reasoning. `scripts/backfill_news_history.py`'s `_fetch_headlines`/`_fetch_earnings_dates` each gained an FMP-first half using the same one-wide-call-then-reconstruct-locally approach the Finnhub path already used. All new settings default to today's exact Finnhub-only behavior; nothing changes until `FMP_NEWS_ENABLED=true` is set. Tests: `tests/test_fmp_news.py`, plus extended coverage in `tests/test_news_catalyst.py`, `tests/test_sentiment_sources.py`, `tests/test_research_brief.py`, `tests/test_backfill_news_history.py`.

- **`universe_engine.py` fix: FMP-primary constituent-changes feed + broadened dead-letter fallback (`settings.FMP_UNIVERSE_ENABLED` default `True` (flipped from False, 2026-08 PR #737), 2026-08 — full reference: `docs/FMP_INTEGRATION.md` §8)**: Wikipedia removed the "Selected changes to the list of S&P 500 components" table from `List_of_S%26P_500_companies` entirely (confirmed live, 2026-08 — the content is gone, not a markup shift), which broke `universe_engine.py::fetch_and_cache_universe()` unconditionally on any fresh clone with no pre-existing `data/universe_cache.parquet`, cascading into 5 failing tests via `validation/harness.py:655`'s unguarded call. Two independent fixes. (1) **FMP as the primary changes source**: `data/fmp_client.py::historical_sp500_changes` + `data/fmp_universe.py::fetch_sp500_changes_via_fmp` (the two-gate `FMP_UNIVERSE_ENABLED`+`FMP_API_KEY` convention, FMP-first/Wikipedia-fallback dispatcher mirroring `fetch_company_headlines`'s FMP/Finnhub pattern in `signals/news_catalyst.py`) — never raises, degrades to `[]` on any failure so `fetch_and_cache_universe()` falls through to the Wikipedia changes-table scrape unchanged. Wikipedia's current-constituents table (confirmed still intact) stays the unconditional source of truth for the CURRENT roster regardless of this flag; only the historical-changes half is FMP-eligible. **Not verified against a live FMP account** — the endpoint path/field names are best-effort from public docs only (this integration was built in a sandbox with no live-market network access and FMP's docs site blocked automated fetches); see `docs/FMP_INTEGRATION.md` §8 for the honest verification-status note before trusting `FMP_UNIVERSE_ENABLED=True` as a working primary path rather than a safe no-op. (2) **Broadened dead-letter fallback, independent of FMP**: `fetch_and_cache_universe()`'s stale-cache fallback previously covered only a network-request exception; the three structural `ValueError`s (too-few-tables, missing-symbol-column, missing-changes-columns — the one actually triggering the live break) now get the same treatment — fall back to a stale cache if one exists, else raise the original exception type/message unchanged. `validation/harness.py::run()`'s call to `get_universe_with_survivorship_warning` is separately guarded (mirroring `simulation_engine.py::print_survivorship_warning_for_backtest`'s pre-existing try/except around the exact same call) and degrades to an honest NaN/`None`-flagged `bias_report` sentinel (CONSTRAINT #4 — never fabricate `n_current`/`estimated_bias_pct`) instead of crashing the whole validation run; `reports/validation_report_template.html.j2` renders that sentinel as an honest "unavailable" note rather than literal "nan"/"None" text. New env var: `FMP_UNIVERSE_ENABLED`. Tests: `tests/test_fmp_universe.py`, plus extended coverage in `tests/test_dead_letter_resilience.py` (`TestFetchAndCacheUniverseMalformedTable`'s new cache-present-fallback cases, `TestFetchAndCacheUniverseFMPPrimarySource`, `TestValidationHarnessUniverseFailureGuard`).

- **`scripts/refresh_validations.py` fix: unthrottled single-shot `yfinance` bulk fetch replaced with per-ticker FMP fetches (2026-08-21)**: the 2026-08-21 universe-widening change above (`_XSEC_UNIVERSE_WIDE`, ~503 tickers) turned `_download_closes()`/`_download_ohlcv()`'s single unthrottled `yf.download()` call for the *entire* universe into a real production failure the first time it actually ran at that width — two separate `python -m scripts.refresh_validations --strategies <29 names>` runs measured Yahoo/yfinance failing 446 of 503 tickers (89%), split between yfinance's generic-empty-response "possibly delisted" message (hitting obviously-real names like SPY/AMZN/MSFT) and yfinance's own local SQLite tz-cache contention (`OperationalError: unable to open database file`, from ~500 concurrent per-ticker threads hammering one WAL-mode cache file — not a Stockpy database). This cascaded into 17 of 29 strategies hard-erroring with a misleading "insufficient history" message (the real cause was a required ticker's column existing in `closes_df` but being all-NaN) and, worse, two "completed" results silently CONTAMINATED by data starvation rather than failing loudly — `sector_quality_rank` reported `deployable=True`/Sharpe=0.80/PBO=0.0/DSR=1.0 while 90-99% of its 100-ticker universe was incorrectly excluded as "microcap" by `signals/multifactor.py`'s `market_cap.fillna(0.0)` fail-closed branch, tripped by missing market-cap data rather than these actually being small caps. Fixed by migrating `_download_closes`/`_download_ohlcv`/`_download_shares` off `yfinance` entirely onto per-ticker `data/fmp_client.py` calls (`historical_eod`/`shares_float`) inside a bounded `ThreadPoolExecutor`, reusing `data/market_data.py`'s existing `_fmp_bars_payload_to_df`/`_warn_once_if_fmp_bars_adjustment_mismatched` reshape/adjustment-convention helpers rather than reimplementing them — pacing comes from FMP's own existing module-level throttle/retry/circuit-breaker (`FMP_MIN_REQUEST_INTERVAL_SECONDS`/`FMP_MAX_RETRIES`/`FMP_COOLDOWN_THRESHOLD`, mirroring the Shared GDELT rate limiter bullet's pattern above) rather than a bespoke chunk-and-sleep loop. Per-ticker failures are still dead-lettered (logged, ticker absent from the result, never fabricated) exactly as before. Also tightened `_validate_single_strategy`'s availability check from bare column presence to `t in closes_df.columns and closes_df[t].notna().any()`, so an all-NaN-but-present column now trips the existing, correctly-worded `RuntimeError` instead of silently passing through to the generic downstream error. Verified: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_refresh_validations.py -q` — 119 passed, 0 failures.

- **Jules coding-agent API integration (`data/jules_client.py`, 2026-08 — full reference: `docs/JULES_INTEGRATION.md`)**: an opt-in, `JULES_ENABLED`-gated (default `False`, a `settings_keysets.DANGEROUS_KEYS` member) integration with Google's third-party autonomous coding agent — given a prompt and a connected GitHub repo/branch, Jules writes code and opens a real, unsupervised PR (hardcoded `automationMode=AUTO_CREATE_PR`; there is no lower-stakes mode). Exposed via `investyo_mcp_server.py`'s `list_jules_sources`/`dispatch_jules_task` MCP tools and `scripts/jules_dispatch.py`'s `list-sources`/`create-session` CLI, both requiring an explicit `confirm=True`/`--confirm` per dispatch (mirroring `gui/orchestrator_runner.HIGH_STAKES_COMMANDS`'s gate for other irreversible actions) plus a same-day dispatch-ledger dedup check (`output/jules_dispatched.jsonl`, `--force`/`force=True` to override). Stated plainly rather than papered over: `confirm=True` is a materially weaker gate than the `robinhood-execution` skill's human-narrated, per-order confirmation loop — what actually closes that gap today is the `.claude/skills/jules-delegation/SKILL.md` skill's prose hard-stop against passing `confirm=True` without the operator's per-task go-ahead, enforced by an agent following the skill, not by code. See `docs/JULES_INTEGRATION.md` for the full setup sequencing and the honest disclosed-unknowns list (Jules's pricing/quota model has not been independently verified as part of this integration).

- **Jules dispatch hardening — prompt-hash pinning + cooldown + verified branch-protection finding (`data/jules_client.py`, 2026-09, Phases 1-3 of `.claude/jules_confirm_hard_gate_implementation_plan.md`; Phase 4 — an out-of-band confirmation channel — scoped 2026-09-08 then explicitly closed, not built)**: **Phase 1** adds `request_dispatch_approval(prompt, source, branch, title) -> {"approval_token", "prompt_hash", "expires_at"}`, which records a durable, single-use, sha256-content-hash-pinned pre-approval in `output/jules_pending_approvals.json` (TTL `settings.JULES_APPROVAL_TTL_SECONDS`, default 600s). `dispatch_session()` now requires a matching, unexpired, unused `approval_token` (a new required-alongside-`confirm` kwarg, threaded through `investyo_mcp_server.py`'s new `request_jules_dispatch_approval` MCP tool + `dispatch_jules_task`'s new `approval_token` param, and `scripts/jules_dispatch.py`'s new `request-approval` subcommand + `create-session --approval-token`), raising `JulesApprovalMismatch(JulesUnavailable)` naming EXACTLY which check failed (missing / unknown / expired / already used / content changed since approval) — never one collapsed message. **Phase 2** adds a dispatch cooldown (`settings.JULES_DISPATCH_COOLDOWN_SECONDS`, default 60s, `0` disables): `dispatch_session()` refuses a repeat dispatch before that many seconds have elapsed since the last successful one, read from the existing dispatch ledger's own last timestamp — no new shared-budget primitive (`JulesDispatchCooldownActive(JulesUnavailable)`). **Both are stated plainly as bar-raising against accidental drift/loops, NOT bypass-proof against a deliberate single-agent-turn action** — an agent with this repo's normal filesystem/shell access can call the approval step and the dispatch step back-to-back with matching content in one turn, exactly as it always could with `confirm=True` alone; see `.claude/jules_confirm_hard_gate_implementation_plan.md` Sec 2 for the research finding this was built from, and `docs/JULES_INTEGRATION.md` Sec 4 for the full, unsoftened statement. **Phase 3** verified (not assumed) GitHub branch-protection status on `kevinmarko/Stockpy`'s `main` branch via a live `gh api` call: protection IS enabled (required status checks, `strict: true`, 1 required approving review) but **`require_code_owner_reviews` is `false`** (a Jules-authored PR touching CODEOWNERS-listed paths is not actually required to get the operator's own review) and **`enforce_admins.enabled` is `false`**, and this very sandbox's own `gh` credentials were confirmed to hold `admin: true` on the repo — meaning an agent with this environment's own GitHub token could bypass the required-review gate entirely via an admin-privileged merge path. See `docs/JULES_INTEGRATION.md` Sec 4 for the full finding; nothing in this pass changed the repo's actual GitHub settings. **Phase 4 (2026-09-08): scoped, then closed rather than built.** Three designs were costed against the plan's own "do not build without a fresh, dedicated scoping pass given its real expense" instruction — a PushNotification-relayed code (found to add no real security: the same session composes and later validates it), an external OTP/TOTP verification service (genuine security, but real ongoing third-party cost the operator would have to set up and maintain), and two-way ntfy action-buttons (same "receiver must live somewhere this session can't touch" requirement as the OTP option, not a cheaper alternative). Closed after the operator asked "why would we need this?": an agent with this repo's normal filesystem/shell access already has far larger levers than Jules dispatch — it can edit `execution/risk_gate.py`, place a live trade, exfiltrate every `.env` credential, or simply remove the check itself before calling `dispatch_session` — so hardening this one narrow MCP tool doesn't proportionally reduce exposure to a genuinely malicious/compromised agent, the same structural finding as the Path A per-agent-identity closure above. The realistic failure mode this integration actually faces (accidental content drift, accidental/looping dispatch) is already covered by Phases 1-3. See `.claude/jules_confirm_hard_gate_implementation_plan.md`'s Phase 4 entry and `docs/JULES_INTEGRATION.md` Sec 6 for the full reasoning. Tests: `tests/test_jules_client.py` (extended — every `dispatch_session` test now obtains an `approval_token` via a `_approve(...)` helper; new `TestDispatchSessionApprovalGate`/`TestRequestDispatchApproval`/`TestDispatchCooldown` classes), `tests/test_jules_dispatch.py` (extended), `tests/test_investyo_mcp_server.py` (extended), `tests/test_jules_delegation_skill.py` (new — pins that `.claude/skills/jules-delegation/SKILL.md` and its `.agents/` mirror still describe both mechanisms honestly as bar-raising, not bypass-proof, mirroring `tests/test_robinhood_e2e.py::TestSkillMdInvariantsPinned`'s pattern for the analogous Robinhood gap). New env vars: `JULES_APPROVAL_TTL_SECONDS`, `JULES_DISPATCH_COOLDOWN_SECONDS`.

- **`NTFY_TOPIC` renamed to `ALERT_NTFY_TOPIC` (2026-08)**: Ntfy push alerts are now actively wired. If you had `ALERT_NTFY_TOPIC` configured, alerts will begin firing (previously was a silent no-op due to a mismatched env var read).

- **Recurring os.environ-bypass/missing-timeout bug classes generalized into real CI gates (PR #962, 2026-08-31 audit+fix pass)**: this repo's own changelog shows the identical bug *shape* recurring across unrelated modules — a real `Settings` field read via bare `os.environ`/`os.getenv` instead of `settings.X` (independently fixed in Finnhub, EDGAR, `market_data.py`, `prompt_registry` — at least 4 prior discoveries) and an external call with no `timeout=` (FRED, then a "comprehensive sweep," then a second independent pipeline hang) — each caught by a one-off audit, never by a structural gate. PR #962 (6 parallel Work Packages, then a 7-agent verify+fix pass) closed the remaining known instances and, more importantly, made both bug classes mechanically un-reintroducible: **(1) `tests/test_measure_settings_census.py`'s `TestFormDOsEnvironIsFullyAllowlisted`** now gates `scripts/measure_settings_census.py`'s existing `form_d_os_environ` census measurement — it asserts the ONLY two remaining `os.environ` reads of anything resembling a Settings-shaped field repo-wide are `GCLOUD_BIN` (`mcp_remote_adapter.py`, explicitly configured via `claude_desktop_config.json`'s `env` block, never `.env`) and `NO_VENV_REEXEC` (`scripts/_bootstrap.py`, read before `settings.py` can safely import — same category as `main.py`'s own reexec guard); `settings.py`'s existing comment block near `RH_LOGIN_WORKER` now cross-references this test as the enforcement mechanism. **The PR's first draft of this test was a non-functional stub** (a ~450-line allowlist literal followed by a bare `assert True`) — caught and replaced with a real assertion during the follow-up verify pass, and live-confirmed to actually fail when a bypass is reintroduced (added-then-reverted a scratch `os.environ.get("WATCHLIST")` call as the regression-catch proof). **(2) `tests/test_no_missing_call_timeouts.py`** is a new AST guard flagging any `subprocess.run/call/check_call/check_output` or `requests.<get|post|put|delete|head|patch|request>` call missing a `timeout=` kwarg, with import-alias resolution (`import subprocess as sp` is still caught) and an explicit, disclosed gap (it cannot safely flag `.wait()`/`.communicate()` on a `Popen` result — that needs real type inference, since `threading.Event`/`Condition`/`Queue` all expose `.wait()` too). **The PR's first draft had zero real alias resolution and was missing `subprocess.call` from its checked-method list** — both silently defeated the check's own stated purpose (the venv-reexec allowlist entries didn't even match real violations) — fixed during the follow-up pass; scanning the full repo post-fix found exactly 4 legitimate venv-reexec allowlist sites (`main.py`, `main_orchestrator.py`, `"Gravity AI Review Suite.py"`, `scripts/_bootstrap.py`), zero other false positives, and zero real un-covered violations. **Also fixed in this pass**: `shared/robinhood_execution_panel.py::ntfy_topic_configured` was missed by the original alerting-subsystem fix above — it still read a bare `NTFY_TOPIC` and its own docstring still claimed that was correct; now reads `settings.ALERT_NTFY_TOPIC` (bool-only return preserved, CONSTRAINT #3). `settings.py`'s `QDRANT_URL`/`QDRANT_COLLECTION` fields were declared with empty-string defaults instead of the original `os.environ.get(key, default)` fallbacks (`http://localhost:6333` / `investyo_news`) during the `agents/rag_orchestrator.py` migration — a real regression, fixed. `tests/test_options_desk_response_contract.py` (the new options-desk mock/live contract test — see the "Options desk mock/live parity sweep" bullets above for why this endpoint family specifically) was verified, not just trusted, to actually catch a regression: a temporary field rename in both the live `api/pilots_api.py` response and in `webapp/src/api/types.ts` were each confirmed to fail the test loudly before being reverted. See `.claude/*_walkthrough.md` files for the full per-Work-Package detail.

- **Sentry error-tracking instrumentation (`observability/sentry_integration.py`)**: a dedicated `init_sentry()` hook for reporting unhandled exceptions from the persistent background orchestrator daemon (which can otherwise fail silently out-of-sight for hours). Gated by `settings.SENTRY_ENABLED` (default `True` — an explicit exception to the "new instrumentation defaults off" convention, because it is provably safe/no-op until `SENTRY_DSN` is also populated, so leaving it on carries zero risk for existing deployments while keeping it discoverable) and requires `sentry-sdk` (added to `requirements-optional.txt`, loaded via lazy import so the daemon still starts up fine without it). **Scope constraint:** this is wired into `desktop/orchestrator_daemon.py`'s entrypoint ONLY — `main.py` and the standalone `api/*.py` services are intentionally unaffected (disclosed, deliberate follow-up).

- **`.env` resolution fix + `scripts/` venv/`.env` bootstrap (`settings.ENV_PATH`, `scripts/_bootstrap.py`, 2026-08)**: root-caused an operator report of a stale-looking `RH_USERNAME` error despite a correctly-populated `.env`. Three independent `.env` locators previously disagreed: pydantic-settings' own `env_file=".env"` (CWD-relative), a bare `load_dotenv()` in `main.py`/`main_orchestrator.py`/`app_shell.py`/`desktop/orchestrator_daemon.py` (walks UP via `find_dotenv()` from the calling file's directory — in a git worktree with no `.env` of its own, this silently found a PARENT checkout's `.env`), and `shared/env_io.py`/`data/brokerage_credentials.py`'s own independently-derived repo-root paths. `settings.ENV_PATH` (`Path(__file__).resolve().parent / ".env"`) is now the SINGLE source every locator imports rather than re-deriving; all four `load_dotenv()` call sites pass it explicitly. Separately, `data/robinhood_portfolio.py::_require_env` (renamed `_require_setting`) and `data/brokerage_credentials.py`'s credential functions read via `os.environ` — the exact "pydantic-settings loads `.env` into `Settings` only, never into the real process environment" bug class this file already documents for Finnhub/EDGAR/Reddit — and now read via `settings.settings.X`; `write_rh_credentials`/`clear_rh_credentials` additionally assign the live settings singleton, not just `.env` and `os.environ`, so the write they just made isn't invisible until restart. The five standalone `api/*.py` FastAPI services (previously the ONLY caller that never loaded `.env` at all, since they have no `main()`-style entry point to hook into) now each call `load_dotenv(ENV_PATH, ...)` at true module top. **`scripts/_bootstrap.py`** (new, stdlib-only at module scope) gives every `scripts/*.py` entry point a `bootstrap()` call mirroring `main.py`'s venv-reexec guard (re-exec under `.venv`'s interpreter if not already there) plus `.env` loading — fixing the OTHER half of the original report (`finnhub-python` was installed only in `.venv`; the operator's system Python lacked it). Placement is split by import risk: the 12 scripts never imported as a library elsewhere call `bootstrap()` at true module top (catches a wrong interpreter before any heavy import crashes); the 6 scripts ALSO imported as a library by production code (`scripts/snapshot_diff.py` by `main_orchestrator.py`/`api/data_api.py`/`pipeline/production_steps.py` and more; `scripts/daily_briefing.py`/`preflight_check.py`/`refresh_validations.py`/`train_lgbm.py`/`train_meta_labelers.py` by Gravity or sibling scripts) call it only inside `if __name__ == "__main__":`, so a plain `import scripts.X` from a running process never risks a subprocess re-exec mid-import — mirroring `main_orchestrator.py`'s own existing `if __name__ == "__main__":`-guarded reexec block, which `main.py`'s unconditional one does not have. `data.portfolio_sync.resolve_universe` gained `allow_live_broker_fetch: bool = True` (threaded to `fetch_account_snapshot`'s new `allow_live_fetch` kwarg) so headless backfill scripts (`backfill_news_history.py`, `backfill_news_history_from_audit.py`, `backfill_sentiment_history.py`, `backfill_edgar_fundamentals.py`) resolve the universe from cache only, never attempting a live Robinhood TOTP/MFA login (which, absent `RH_MFA_SECRET`, falls back to a blocking interactive prompt or raises immediately headless). Tests: `tests/test_env_loading.py`, `tests/test_robinhood_portfolio.py`, `tests/test_brokerage_connect.py`, `tests/test_robinhood_client.py`, `tests/test_portfolio_sync.py`, `tests/test_run_once.py`, `tests/test_backfill_edgar_fundamentals.py`.

- **Runtime settings store — READ path (`runtime_flags.py`, 2026-08)**: lets a `Settings` field value be sourced from `output/runtime_flags.json` in addition to real env vars and `.env`. **Zero behavioral change until that file exists** — no writer ships with it (that is a separate, later task), and no install has the file, so `apply_overrides` is a no-op everywhere today. Precedence is **real shell env > store > `.env` > field default**. Four rules govern any change here. (1) **`runtime_flags.py` is a stdlib-only leaf imported BY `settings.py`** (at the very bottom, after `settings = Settings()`), so it must NEVER import `settings`/`shared.env_io`/`config` at any scope — that is a circular import that breaks `import settings` for all ~146 dependent modules; `settings_keysets.py` (itself importing nothing but `__future__`) is the one permitted project import, and `python-dotenv` is imported lazily inside the one function needing it. AST-enforced by `tests/test_runtime_flags.py::TestModuleIsADependencyFreeLeaf`. (2) **Assignment goes through `Settings.__pydantic_validator__.validate_assignment`, never `pydantic.TypeAdapter`** — `TypeAdapter` coerces to the annotated type but silently BYPASSES `@field_validator`s, verified: it passes `ROBINHOOD_EXECUTION_MODE="garbage-value"` straight through, where the real validator collapses anything outside `{off, review, live}` to the inert `off` precisely so a bad value can never arm live execution. Same mechanism as `api/pilots_api.py`'s `PUT /llm/setting`. (3) **`settings_keysets.BOOTSTRAP_KEYS` is an absolute exclusion** — a stored value for one of those 7 fields is skipped with a WARNING, never applied. (4) **Path anchoring is `Path(__file__).resolve().parent`**, re-derived independently (it cannot import `settings.ENV_PATH`/`OUTPUT_DIR` — `OUTPUT_DIR` is itself a `BOOTSTRAP_KEY` for that self-referential reason) and pinned equal to `ENV_PATH` by a test in BOTH `tests/test_runtime_flags.py` and `tests/test_env_loading.py`; anything CWD-relative or `find_dotenv()`-style would reintroduce the parent-worktree bug the bullet above documents. **JSON shape the not-yet-built writer must produce** — `{"version": 1, "flags": {"FIELD": {"value": <json>, ...optional audit metadata}}}`; the `{"value": ...}` envelope is REQUIRED, not optional, because `SECTOR_FORECAST_CONFIGS` is `dict`-typed and a bare-scalar form would be ambiguous with a dict field's real value. Failure handling is dead-letter-per-key (CONSTRAINT #6): a corrupt file / wrong `version` / non-object `flags` applies nothing and logs a WARNING, while an unknown field name, a `BOOTSTRAP_KEYS` member, an env-pinned name, or a value failing validation skips that ONE key and applies the rest. **Never logs a stored value** — a store entry may hold a credential, and `str(ValidationError)` embeds `input_value=` (verified), so only `errors()[0]["msg"]` is ever logged. Known gap left to the writer: this module does not refuse `shared/env_io.py::SECRET_KEYS` (it cannot import that set; duplicating an 80-key credential list into a leaf is a worse drift hazard) — refusing to WRITE a secret belongs in the writer. `settings.RUNTIME_FLAGS_REPORT` exposes the per-run `ApplyReport` (`applied`/`skipped_bootstrap`/`skipped_env_pinned`/`skipped_unknown`/`skipped_invalid`/`error`) for a later operator surface; nothing reads it to make a decision. Tests: `tests/test_runtime_flags.py`, `tests/test_env_loading.py::TestEnvPathAnchor`.

- **Runtime settings store — WRITE path (`runtime_flags_writer.py`, 2026-08)**: the other half of the bullet above, and **the first and only thing in this codebase that writes `output/runtime_flags.json`** — until it existed the read path was provably inert because no writer did. Two entry points, both of which NEVER raise (CONSTRAINT #6) and always return a frozen `WriteResult(key, ok, applied_value, reason, persisted, applies)`: `write_override(key, raw_value, *, actor="", path=None)` and `delete_override(key, *, actor="", path=None)`. **This module is deliberately NOT a stdlib-only leaf and must NEVER be imported by `settings.py` or `runtime_flags.py`** (source-scan enforced by `tests/test_runtime_flags_writer.py::TestModuleWiring`) — it is only ever called from request-handling code running long after `settings.py` finished importing, which is exactly what lets it import `shared.env_io` and close the one gap `runtime_flags.py`'s own docstring names as left to the writer: refusing to persist a `SECRET_KEYS` field. **Five refusal gates, in order**: (1) `shared.env_io.SECRET_KEYS`; (2) `settings_keysets.BOOTSTRAP_KEYS`, with the per-field string from `BOOTSTRAP_KEY_REASONS` as the reason, not a generic message; (3) not a real `Settings.model_fields` name (`extra="ignore"` would otherwise store a typo forever and skip it forever); (4) validation via `Settings.__pydantic_validator__.validate_assignment`, never `TypeAdapter`; (5) the COERCED value must survive `json.dumps`. Gate 1 runs before gate 3 because 38 of the 40 secret keys ARE real fields, so ordering is what makes the refusal attributable to secrecy; and the two key sets **overlap on `DATABASE_URL`/`MCP_DATABASE_URL_RO`**, where gate 1 wins — a DSN can embed credentials, and "this is a secret" is the stronger statement (`settings_keysets.py`'s own `DATABASE_URL` reason says as much). Deliberately NOT gates: `ALLOWED_KEYS` (that is the `.env` writer's allowlist for a different, durable-at-next-launch mechanism) and `DANGEROUS_KEYS` (needs an operator confirmation step, which is the calling layer's concern — this module could not tell "the operator confirmed" from "the caller forgot to ask"). **Validation runs on `settings.settings.model_copy()`, not the live singleton** (verified: `model_copy` gives a distinct `__dict__`), so a refusal at any gate leaves the process byte-identical, and the live apply is delegated to `runtime_flags.apply_overrides` — meaning the singleton is mutated by exactly ONE code path and precedence (real shell env > store > `.env` > default) has exactly one implementation the writer cannot drift from. **`applies`** is `"immediately"` | `"next_daemon_restart"` | `"env_pinned"` | `"refused"`; `"immediately"` honestly means only that the singleton's attribute moved — the 78 `restart_required` fields in `docs/settings_liveness.json` still need a restart before the change has real effect, and that per-field classification is deliberately left to the calling UI rather than duplicated here. An **env-pinned** write still persists (durable for a future unpinned process) but reports `applies="env_pinned"` with `applied_value` set to the *env-sourced value that won*, never the value written, so the gap is visible instead of a false `"immediately"`. **Concurrency** is scoped rigor for a single-operator artifact: a module-level `threading.RLock` serializes read-merge-write-replace so concurrent threads in one process (FastAPI runs `def` handlers in a threadpool — the realistic race) cannot lose each other's keys, the existing file is re-read INSIDE the lock immediately before the merge, and the file is replaced via temp + `os.replace` (same idiom as `shared/env_io.py::write_many_atomic`); a cross-PROCESS writer landing between our read and our replace is still last-writer-wins, the same residual `write_many_atomic` documents for `.env` and not made worse here. **A damaged store splits two ways**: unparseable/wrong-shape is renamed to `runtime_flags.json.corrupt.<UTC>` (bytes preserved for hand-recovery) and a fresh store written with a WARNING; an unsupported schema `version` is REFUSED outright, because that is not corruption but a valid file from a NEWER build, and overwriting it with a v1 file would destroy current state this build merely cannot read. The merge re-reads the raw file rather than rebuilding from `load_store()`, which unwraps the `{"value": ...}` envelope and would restamp every untouched key's `updated_at`/`updated_by` — destroying provenance the call was never asked to touch. **Audit log**: `output/runtime_flags_audit.jsonl`, always a SIBLING of the store (so redirecting the store path in a test redirects the audit trail too), one JSON line per call including refusals. It **never records the value, even on success** — `_append_audit` has no parameter that could carry one, so leaking is structurally impossible rather than a matter of discipline (a field not formally in `SECRET_KEYS` can still receive sensitive-shaped input). If the store write succeeds but the audit append fails the call still reports `ok=True`/`persisted=True` — the value genuinely IS persisted and live, and reporting failure would invite a retry that double-writes — with the gap logged at ERROR. `delete_override` applies **no** classification gates (deletion can only reduce what the store overrides, so refusing would only strand a hand-added entry the read path keeps applying) and reverts the live value by constructing a fresh `Settings()`, because the read path only APPLIES stored keys and has no memory of what a field held before an override landed, possibly in a previous process. Tests: `tests/test_runtime_flags_writer.py` (104 tests; 9 hand-run mutations, 8 confirmed caught — removing the secret gate, validating on the live singleton instead of a copy, `str(exc)` in place of `errors()[0]["msg"]`, dropping the write lock, and giving `_append_audit` a value-carrying parameter among them). The 9th — **replacing the atomic temp-file + `os.replace` with a direct in-place `store.write_text`** — was initially NOT caught: all 100 other tests stayed green, i.e. the atomicity claim was documented but unenforced. `TestAtomicWrite` closes that gap, pinning that exactly one `os.replace` publishes the file and its source is a temp SIBLING (same directory, or the rename is a cross-filesystem copy and no longer atomic), and that a failure at the publish step leaves the previous store byte-identical and still loadable by the read path rather than truncated.

- **Mission Control research-driven overhaul (`webapp/src/screens/Observability.tsx`, `webapp/src/screens/Dashboard.tsx`, `market_data_latency.py`, 2026-08)**: a structured operator interview (the platform's single real user) plus a heuristic/code audit found the screen's actual problem wasn't missing sections — it was that it had never earned a reason to be opened ("I really don't check it. I don't know what it does."). Fixes, in priority order: (1) a `sizing cap`/`etf transmission` GLOSSARY entry (`webapp/src/help/helpContent.ts`) plus a per-section `?` `InfoTip` affordance on `SectionHeading` (`helpKey` prop) — the one-shot, first-visit-only `TabGuide` panel was the screen's ONLY explanation surface, and it never covered several sections at all; (2) a client-side-only `AttentionStripSection` at the top of the page, derived by the pure `webapp/src/observabilityAttention.ts::deriveAttentionItems()` from fields the composite endpoint already returns (circuit-breaker counts, portfolio-heat over-limit, risk-gate-block count, a sizing-cap **escalation** event specifically — not routine capping, which CLAUDE.md already documents as "routine for an established Kelly book" — a stale >120s heartbeat, and the macro gate being off), rendering an honest "All clear" when nothing qualifies; the page's only two real controls (the macro-gate toggle, the ETF-transmission config link) were relocated above the fold into a visually distinct "control" card and given `Button variant="primary"` instead of blending into the surrounding read-only tiles; (3) every section past the attention strip/portfolio-risk/equity is now collapsed by default inside a native `<details>` "Background telemetry" disclosure; (4) `Dashboard.tsx` (the app's actual `/` landing route) runs the SAME `deriveAttentionItems()` over its own `GET /observability/summary` fetch and shows a `Notice` banner linking to `/observability` ONLY when non-empty — the cross-screen trigger the page never had. Two previously-confirmed real gaps against the retired Streamlit panel were also closed: a **per-symbol forecast-skill table** (`pilots/observability.py::forecast_skill_by_symbol_summary` — a bulk `GROUP BY symbol, horizon_days, model_name` SQL aggregate reproducing `ForecastTracker.get_skill_weights`'s exact cold-start/inverse-RMSE formula per symbol, replacing the legacy panel's N×4×3 per-cell round-trip loop with three bulk queries) and a **per-symbol data-latency heatmap**. The latency heatmap is a REPLACEMENT, not a literal port: the legacy `LatencySampleStore` lived only in Streamlit `st.session_state`, populated only on a manual "Fetch quotes" click — a stateless FastAPI process has no equivalent session, and CONSTRAINT #4 forbids fabricating a history that isn't real (the same reasoning that already kept the Heartbeat Age Trend sparkline unported). The new `market_data_latency.py` (stdlib-only, zero project imports, matching the "flat, modular Engine architecture" convention) is a thread-safe, fixed-capacity in-process ring buffer, recorded AUTOMATICALLY on every real (non-cache-hit) fetch through `data/market_data.py::CompositeProvider.get_latest_quote` — strictly more useful than the manual-trigger original, not a downgrade — gated behind new setting **`MARKET_DATA_LATENCY_TRACKING_ENABLED`** (bool, default `False`, matching this codebase's dominant convention that new instrumentation defaults off even when read-only/diagnostic). Never persisted to disk, so a restarted API process honestly reports zero samples rather than a stale cross-restart figure (`pilots/observability.py::latency_heatmap_summary` surfaces this the same way `heartbeat_summary` already does). Both new sections follow the existing `sizing_cap_audit`/`etf_transmission` end-to-end template exactly: a new `ObservabilitySummary` key on the one composite `GET /observability/summary` call (no new endpoint, no `client.ts` changes), a happy-path + exported honest-disabled mock builder pair in `mock.ts`, and matching `Observability.test.tsx` coverage. All new/changed settings default to today's exact behavior. Tests: `tests/test_market_data_latency.py`, `tests/test_pilots_observability.py::TestForecastSkillBySymbol`/`TestLatencyHeatmapSummary`, `tests/test_market_data.py::TestCompositeProviderLatencyTracking`, plus the webapp additions above.

- **Options desk Tier A/B/C/D build-out — Phases 19-30 of `.claude/giant_master_plan.md`, 2026-08 (PR #744, #750, #751, #753)**: `pilots/lob_simulator.py` (Phase 19, Cont-Stoikov-Talreja L3 queue sim, real Gillespie/Markov-jump Monte Carlo), `pilots/options_gex.py` (Phase 20, dealer gamma exposure/walls/zero-flip, closed-form BS Γ with verified sign convention), `pilots/copula_stat_arb.py` (Phase 21, real Clayton/Gumbel/Frank/Gaussian MLE copula fitting + Kalman-filter dynamic hedge ratio — the strongest-tested module of the whole build-out, the only one with a genuine lookahead-perturbation test), `ml/drl_market_maker.py` (Phase 22), `ml/transformer_vol_forecaster.py` (Phase 23), `validation/synthetic_diffusion_engine.py` (Phase 24), `sizing/hrp_cvar_optimizer.py` (Phase 25, real López de Prado HRP quasi-diagonalization + Rockafellar-Uryasev CVaR via SLSQP), `execution/almgren_chriss_router.py` (Phase 26, real sinh/cosh optimal trajectory), `execution/fix_gateway.py` (Phase 27, real FIX 4.4 `NewOrderSingle`/`ExecutionReport`/`OrderCancelReplace` state machine, multi-venue `VenueConfig` aggregation), `llm/research_copilot.py` + `validation/autonomous_backtest_runner.py` (Phase 28, AST-safe LLM signal synthesis with a real adversarial-tested sandbox — dunder-access/forbidden-import/forbidden-call rejection — plus genuine CPCV backtest evaluation), `webapp/src/components/charts/VolSurface3D.tsx`/`LobDepth3D.tsx` (Phase 29), `execution/multi_broker_gateway.py` + `execution/sec_rule_606_reporter.py` + `data/execution_audit_store.py` (Phase 30, a real `CircuitState.CLOSED/OPEN/HALF_OPEN` failover state machine and genuine NBBO price-improvement math) all exist and are substantively implemented — this is real, tested engineering, not stubs. Also shipped but numbered outside the plan's own phase scheme: `pilots/multi_leg_pricing.py` (real Black-Scholes multi-leg pricer with 0DTE/zero-vol guards; its introducing PR loosely labeled it "Phase 21-22," which doesn't match `.claude/giant_master_plan.md`'s actual Phase 21 (copula)/22 (DRL) modules — both of which exist separately and correctly). **A full independent audit (`.claude/giant_master_plan_audit.md`, 2026-08-17) found the code is real but several load-bearing pieces didn't do what they claimed once you looked past the file existing.** Most of that audit's findings were fixed within days (PR #765 "close 7 code-level findings", PR #766 "close F1 -- mock/live API parity", PR #771 "close remaining F3/F7 findings") — re-verified fresh 2026-08-19, current state: (1) Phase 22's "Deep RL (PPO)" claim was true of `train_market_maker_policy` (still a 2-parameter heuristic hill-climb, called by `api/pilots_api.py` via `POST /pilots/options/market-maker/train` since PR #788 but with no webapp caller) but is now **partially closed**: `ml/drl_market_maker_ppo.py` (2026-08-19) is a real actor-critic PPO agent — hand-derived backprop verified against finite-difference gradients, GAE, clipped surrogate objective, a genuinely state-dependent `[delta_bid, delta_ask]` policy — implemented in pure NumPy (not `torch`, which turned out not to be installed anywhere in this environment despite being listed in `requirements-optional.txt`). See `docs/VALIDATION_STRATEGY_FIX_LOG.md`'s 2026-08-19 entry for the honest first-training-run comparison against the closed-form quoter (higher PnL/Sharpe, but also higher inventory risk — not a clean win, a real reference point). Not yet wired to any API endpoint, webapp screen, or `STRATEGY_REGISTRY` entry — that remains a separate follow-up. (2) Phase 29's "WebGL/Three.js" claim was fixed in PR #765 (F9) — `VolSurface3D.tsx`/`LobDepth3D.tsx` still render via Canvas 2D (no `three` package, no real WebGL draw calls — this was never changed), but the misleading "WebGL 3D Active" label was corrected to "Canvas 3D Renderer"/"Canvas 2.5D Renderer" with an honest fallback-mode variant; the Three.js-specific dispose helpers in `threeDisposal.ts` remain unused dead code (cosmetic, not a bug). (3) `execution/fix_recovery.py` was deleted in PR #765 (F8) as superseded dead code — its gap-fill/resend logic is confirmed correctly and more thoroughly covered inline in `execution/fix_gateway.py` (`tests/test_fix_gateway.py::test_sequence_gap_detection_and_automatic_resend`/`test_peer_resend_request_handling`/`test_sequence_reset_reset_mode`). (4) Fixed in PR #771 (F3/F7): `tests/test_transformer_vol_forecaster.py` and `tests/test_synthetic_diffusion_engine.py` both now have genuine walk-forward lookahead-perturbation tests (matching this file's established mutate-future/assert-bit-exact-past convention), and both live `api/pilots_api.py` endpoints (`GET .../transformer-forecast`, `POST .../diffusion-stress-test`) now train on real `HistoricalStore` bars, degrading to an honest HTTP 422 on thin history rather than fabricating `np.random.randn` tensors. (5) Fixed 2026-08-19: `ml/drl_market_maker.py` (Phase 22) has a documented validation exemption (`docs/VALIDATION_STRATEGY_FIX_LOG.md`'s "2026-08: High-Frequency Market Maker (Avellaneda-Stoikov) Validation Exemption" entry) now tightened to state explicitly WHY PBO/DSR don't apply — a 2-parameter hill-climb over one fixed closed-form policy isn't the strategy-selection process PBO measures overfitting risk for, a structural mismatch rather than an overfitting-immunity claim; it stays unregistered in `STRATEGY_REGISTRY` by design. `pilots/copula_stat_arb.py` (Phase 21) now HAS a real `STRATEGY_REGISTRY["copula_stat_arb"]` entry (`_build_copula_stat_arb_adapter`, calling `generate_copula_stat_arb_signals` on a KO/PEP pair, 2005-2026 real data) — measured `Sharpe=-0.455`, `PBO=0.000`, `DSR=0.246`, `MaxDD=35.1%`, `deployable=False`, an honest FAIL (worst drawdown lands on 2008-10-13, the GFC) documented in full in `docs/VALIDATION_STRATEGY_FIX_LOG.md`'s 2026-08-19 entry and `docs/signals/copula_stat_arb.md`'s corrected "Current Status," replacing the borrowed-`pairs_trading`-numbers documentation error. (6) Fixed in PR #765 (F10): the AST import-boundary guard (`tests/test_pilots_strategy_matrix.py`) is no longer a hardcoded allowlist — it now auto-discovers via `Path.glob("*.py")`, covering 55 of 61 `pilots/*.py` modules (6 explicitly exempted with documented per-module reasons), not the stale "3 of 18."

- **Options-desk mock/live parity sweep, round 3 — 4 more confirmed bugs, all fixed and verified (2026-08-29)**: a follow-up audit specifically hunting the "backend signals a real failure/gate, frontend silently mishandles it" bug class (same family as the earnings_crush/dispersion_trading/zero_dte_engine override-affordance fix earlier this file documents), scoped to `PaperBroker.tsx` and adjacent options-desk screens. (1) **`PaperBroker.tsx`'s strategy-options/manage-exits dead-letter fabrication**: `POST /pilots/paper-broker/strategy-options/execute` and `/manage-exits` both dead-letter a real server-side exception into a 200-status `{"ok": false, "error": "..."}` body (CONSTRAINT #6) — which never throws, so `handleExecuteStrategyOptions`/`handleManageExits` read it as a real success and rendered *"Successfully executed undefined strategy trades (undefined skipped, undefined failed)"* / *"Evaluated undefined positions: closed undefined."* to the operator. Fixed via a shared `PaperBrokerDeadLetterResult` union member on both result types (`webapp/src/api/types.ts`) and a new `execDeadLetterError` state routed into the screen's existing red error box, distinct from `execStatus`'s unconditionally-green success banner. **A second, deeper bug found in the same investigation**: `ManageExitsResult`'s declared shape (`evaluated_count`/`closed_count`/`closed_positions`/`message`) never matched the real backend at all, on EITHER path — `pilots/paper_broker.py::manage_position_exits()` returns `execution/options_paper_executor.py::OptionsPaperExecutor.execute_auto_exits()`'s actual shape verbatim (`enabled`/`evaluated_count`/`executed_count`/`failed_count`/`executed`/`failed`, confirmed against `tests/test_options_lifecycle.py`'s own assertions) — meaning the manage-exits status message had never once displayed a correct number on a real backend, success or failure. `ClosedExitPosition` (only ever referenced by the wrong type) was removed as dead/fabricated. (2) **`POST /pilots/options/alerts/test` type/implementation mismatch**: `pilots/options_alerts.py::dispatch_options_alert()`'s real return shape (`status`/`alert_type`/`level`/`title`/`message`/`payload`/`timestamp`/`success`/`error`) shared zero field names with the declared `OptionsAlertTestResult` (`ok`/`dispatched_count`/`channels[]`/per-channel `results[]`) — a real (non-mocked) call crashed `VolForecastScanner.tsx`'s render (`testAlertResult.channels.join(", ")` on `undefined`). Fixed by correcting the frontend type/component to the real, honest shape rather than having the backend fabricate a per-channel breakdown it structurally cannot measure (`dispatch_options_alert` calls `send_alert()` once, all-channels-at-once, with only one aggregate success/error — synthesizing a per-channel status would itself be a CONSTRAINT #4 violation). (3) **`TopStatusBar.tsx`'s job-cancel button silently ignores a disclosed `cancelled: false`**: `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel` honestly reports `cancelled: false` (200, not an error) when it could ask but not confirm the job actually stopped (`api/control_api.py`'s own docstring) — `Console.tsx`/`RunCommandControl.tsx` both already branch on this correctly, but `TopStatusBar.tsx`'s Cancel button only had a `catch` for a thrown exception and silently did nothing on an honest `cancelled: false`. Fixed to match the other two screens' handling (a toast on the unconfirmed case). (4) **`UnusualFlowFeed.tsx` drops two real backend honesty fields (PR #894)**: `pilots/unusual_options_flow.py`'s `price_is_estimated`/`spot_price_is_estimated` (set when a trade's price/spot came from a bid/ask-midpoint or median-strike fallback rather than a real observed value) and the endpoint's `degraded`/`symbols_fetch_failed` (a genuinely incomplete scan, per the endpoint's own docstring) were declared on `UnusualOptionTrade`/`UnusualOptionsFlowResponse` in `types.ts` but never once read by the component — an estimated price rendered with identical visual confidence to a real one (the exact CONSTRAINT #4 failure mode these fields were added to prevent), and an incomplete scan rendered identically to "nothing unusual found." Fixed via a page-level "Incomplete Scan" honesty banner (mirroring `GexProfileView.tsx`'s `chain_source` banner) and small "(est.)" badges next to an estimated fill price/spot price/notional. `mock.ts` also never exercised any of these four fields on the happy path in any of the four bugs above (`managePaperOptionsExits`'s mock fixture matched the same wrong `ManageExitsResult` shape as the type; `testOptionsAlert`'s mock fabricated the same fictional per-channel breakdown; `getUnusualOptionsFlow`'s mock never set the two honesty flags) — all three now match the corrected, real contracts, so mock-mode development actually exercises what live returns. Tests: extended `PaperBroker.test.tsx` (+2), `VolForecastScanner.test.tsx` (+2), `TopStatusBar.test.tsx` (+1), `UnusualFlowFeed.test.tsx` (+3).

- **NotebookLM modular multi-source knowledge pack (`scripts/export_notebooklm.py`, `docs/GOOGLE_NOTEBOOK_INTEGRATION.md`, 2026-09)**: `scripts/export_notebooklm.py` now also writes 5 separate, topic-scoped Markdown documents under `output/notebooklm/` (`01_macro_and_regime.md`, `02_portfolio_and_greeks.md`, `03_strategy_signals_and_picks.md`, `04_trade_journal_and_ledger.md`, `05_options_directives_and_matrix.md`) alongside the existing consolidated `output/notebooklm_source.md`, so an operator uploading sources to Google NotebookLM gets better-focused per-topic grounding (NotebookLM supports up to 50 sources/notebook). New CLI flags: `--output-dir`, `--modular-only`, `--consolidated-only`, `--section {macro,portfolio,signals,trades,options}`. This was rebuilt from scratch on top of `main`'s already-shipped single-file exporter after a 6-agent audit found a separate, isolated prior attempt at this same feature had gone stale relative to `main` (missed PRs #965/#968/#971/#986/#991) and shipped 7 real bugs its own test suite never caught (every fixture was hand-shaped to an invented data shape rather than derived from the real producers — `reporting/state_snapshot.py`, `technical_options_engine.py`, `pilots/trade_history.py`). This rebuild (8 parallel agents: one per generator, one for shared helpers/driver, one for tests, one for docs) fixes all 7, plus 3 more found only once every piece was integrated and run together: (1) **portfolio-Greeks fabrication (CRITICAL)** — the abandoned attempt called `pilots.options_risk.calculate_portfolio_greeks()` with zero arguments, which silently falls into that function's own "no positions" branch and returns an all-zero-looking portfolio regardless of real holdings (CONSTRAINT #4); fixed by delegating to the existing, already-correct `pilots.paper_broker.get_portfolio_greeks()` (resolves a real `PaperAccountStore` + SPY spot quote first). (2) **no crash isolation between generators (CRITICAL)** — one modular generator's uncaught exception used to abort the whole script and silently drop every section scheduled after it; fixed by wrapping each of the 5 modular generator calls in its own try/except in `build_export()`, writing an honest one-line fallback file for just the failing section while every sibling still generates normally. (3) a dead `True_IVR`/`IVR_Proxy` fallback (`dict.get(key, default)`'s `default` never fires when the key is present-but-`None`, which is the common shape `technical_options_engine.py` produces) silently showed "N/A" for a column that usually has a real value; fixed via an explicit `_resolve_ivr()` None/NaN check. (4) a new `_md_escape(value, default="N/A")` helper (escapes `|` → `\|`, collapses embedded newlines) is applied to every free-text value placed in a Markdown table cell (buy/sell ranges, news headlines) — an unescaped `|` in a real `buy_range` string like `"Trim @ $13.30 | Stop @ $13.07"` previously shifted every column after it with no error raised. (5) a shared `_is_missing()` helper (guarded `pandas.isna()`) now also catches `pandas.NA`/`pandas.NaT`, which the old bare `isinstance(value, float)` NaN check let through as literal `"<NA>"`/`"NaT"` garbage. (6) the trade-journal generator now distinguishes "the durable broker-fills store has never been ingested" (`available: False`) from "ingested and genuinely found zero closed trades" (`available: True, n_trades: 0`) — previously identical text for two structurally different situations. **Found only during integration, after all 8 agents' pieces were assembled and the FULL test suite + a real end-to-end run were exercised together** (no single agent's isolated slice could have caught these): (7) the driver initially passed the WRITE-target directory (`output/notebooklm/`) as the READ-source directory to the 3 generators that load `state_snapshot.json`/`options_matrix.json`, so they always silently degraded to "unavailable" even with real upstream data on disk; (8) merging 8 independently-written `_fmt_money` copies into one canonical helper accidentally added a `try/except` that swallowed a corrupted value instead of letting it raise, breaking the `TestPartialAppendProtection` "one bad item degrades the WHOLE section honestly" contract — reverted. A privacy-disclosure gap in the abandoned attempt's docs (never stating that uploading these files sends real equity/positions/P&L/Greeks/options-strike data to Google's cloud) is closed via a freshly-written `docs/GOOGLE_NOTEBOOK_INTEGRATION.md` with a prominent early "Privacy & Data Handling" section. Tests: `tests/test_export_notebooklm.py` — 97 passing (29 pre-existing consolidated-export tests unchanged + 68 new, covering all 10 bugs above plus per-generator happy/degraded-path coverage and CLI-flag filtering).

- **Forecast Backfill Meta-Labeler Bridge (`ml/forecast_backfill_registry_bridge.py`, 2026-09-06, built by Antigravity, audited and fixed by 6 parallel Claude Code agents)**: wires the Forecast Backfill screen's (`ml/forecast_backfill.py::AgenticForecastBackfiller`) own trained RandomForest meta-labelers into the SAME PBO/DSR-gated live registry (`ml/registry.yaml` → `ml/meta_bootstrap.py::bootstrap_meta_registry()` → `signals/aggregator.py`'s meta-label confidence gate) that `scripts/train_meta_labelers.py` already feeds for 2 AFML-trained signals — extending live meta-label coverage to (an operator-opted-in subset of) 6 signals, via a new `meta_labeler_backfill_<signal_id>` registry key namespace and an AFML-priority tie-break (the existing `meta_labeler_<signal_id>` entry wins when both exist and are deployable). Gated behind `settings.META_LABELING_BACKFILL_BRIDGE_ENABLED` (default `False`) plus an explicit per-signal `settings.META_LABELING_BACKFILL_ELIGIBLE_SIGNALS` opt-in (default empty) — fully inert by default, byte-identical to pre-bridge behavior for the 2 existing AFML signals and the screen's own diagnostic CSV/JSON output. Ships with a fail-closed feature-compatibility gate (`LIVE_ROW_FEATURE_WHITELIST`/`check_feature_compatibility`, hand-copied from `strategy_engine.py`'s real live per-ticker `row` construction plus the aggregator's own appended `primary_score` key) that refuses to register any model whose declared training features aren't fully present in the live feature row — applied both at registration time and again at bootstrap time (defense in depth). **As shipped by the initial build, the audit found this bridge was completely non-functional** — `register_backfill_model` unconditionally raised `ImportError` (`from config import MODELS_DIR`, an attribute that doesn't exist) and, past that, called `ml.registry_io.update_model_metrics` with a wrong kwarg (`registry_path` instead of `path`) and a plain string instead of the required `train_window` dict; `compute_backfill_cpcv_metrics` called `validation.metrics.run_cpcv_evaluation` with entirely nonexistent kwargs (`signal_sign`/`target`/`dates`/`horizon_days`/`theta_c`) and a `strategy_fn` whose arity/return-shape didn't match that function's real contract, so DSR/PBO could never be genuinely computed; and step 5's own always-on diagnostic pickle writes (independent of the bridge) still collided with `MetaLabeler.load_latest()`'s glob one level down inside the very `backfill_meta_` namespace introduced to fix the ORIGINAL AFML-vs-screen collision (the shipped fix correctly separated AFML from screen pickles, but step 5's per-horizon diagnostic dumps and step 7's registered model shared one `backfill_meta_{signal_id}_*` glob, so `sorted()[-1]` could pick the wrong, wrong-typed file). All caught only because every test touching this path monkeypatched away the exact two functions that were broken — a real (non-mocked) call was never exercised until the audit ran one directly. Every finding was fixed in the same pass: `compute_backfill_cpcv_metrics` rebuilt to genuinely reuse `run_cpcv_evaluation` (multi-candidate `strategy_fn`, real `t1` purge window, matching `scripts/train_meta_labelers.py::compute_cpcv_metrics`'s validated pattern); `register_backfill_model` fixed to resolve the models directory from `ml.meta_labeling._MODELS_DIR` and call `update_model_metrics` with the correct kwargs; step 5's diagnostic pickles renamed to a third, disjoint `backfill_diag_` prefix (reserving `backfill_meta_` exclusively for step 7's registry-tracked, `MetaLabeler`-wrapped artifacts); `LIVE_ROW_FEATURE_WHITELIST` itself was found missing `primary_score` (a real feature every existing AFML meta-labeler actually trains on, appended by the aggregator after `strategy_engine.py`'s own `row` is built) despite 4 independent audit agents having verified it as an "exact match" — caught by a new drift-detection test that parses `strategy_engine.py`'s real `row` construction directly rather than trusting a hand-verified claim; and the 6 new `ml/registry.yaml` stub rows had `path`/`trained_date` set to `''` instead of honest `null`, and the file's entire ~35-line schema-documentation header comment block had been silently dropped (a `yaml.dump`/`yaml.safe_dump` re-serialization artifact, not a deliberate edit) — both restored. Also fixed: the new `registry_bridge` worker phase (`ml/forecast_backfill_worker.py`) was added unconditionally (regardless of the bridge flag) but never threaded into the webapp's `ForecastBackfillPhase` type union, `PHASE_LABEL` map, or mock simulation, so every real (live-backend) backfill run showed a broken `undefined` progress label at step 7 of 8 — a genuine, if cosmetic, default-path regression, now fixed in all three places plus `ml/forecast_backfill_job.py`'s Python `Literal`. **Disclosed, not fixed here**: even with every bug above fixed, the feature-compatibility gate genuinely and correctly refuses all 6 eligible signals today, since none of their declared `meta_label_features` are fully covered by the live row — this bridge remains fully wired, tested, and safe, but functionally inert until a separate follow-up widens `strategy_engine.py`'s live row schema. New settings: `META_LABELING_BACKFILL_BRIDGE_ENABLED`, `META_LABELING_BACKFILL_ELIGIBLE_SIGNALS`, `META_LABELING_BACKFILL_DEFAULT_HORIZON_DAYS`, `META_LABELING_BACKFILL_LIVE_HORIZON_DAYS`. Tests: `tests/test_train_meta_labelers.py` (AFML-priority tie-break, feature-gate refusal, the `LIVE_ROW_FEATURE_WHITELIST` drift-detection regression, and real-unmocked `compute_backfill_cpcv_metrics`/`register_backfill_model` coverage — the exact gap that let the original bugs ship), `tests/test_forecast_backfill.py` (step 7 wiring, the `backfill_diag_`-vs-`backfill_meta_` intra-namespace collision regression), `tests/test_meta_labeling.py` (`load_latest` prefix-namespace isolation), `tests/test_registry_load.py`, plus extended `webapp/src/screens/ForecastBackfillScreen.test.tsx` coverage for the new Live Registry/CPCV DSR/PBO columns and the corrected, non-overclaiming disclaimer text. **Follow-up fix (2026-09-06, 4-agent pass)**: the feature-compatibility gate above previously refused all 6 eligible signals by construction, because 9 feature names those signals declare in `meta_label_features` (`Vol_20`, `Vol_50`, `Vol_Ratio`, `RSI_14`, `MACD`, `MACD_Signal`, `ROC_6M`, `ROC_5`, `ROC_20`) were absent from `strategy_engine.py`'s live `row`. Fixed: all 9 are now genuinely computed (`processing_engine.py::calculate_technical_metrics()`, plus a training-time fix in `ml/forecast_backfill.py::step_2_calculate_technical_features()` so `options_flow_sentiment`'s previously-silently-dropped `ROC_5`/`ROC_20` are now actually used at training time too) and threaded through `evaluate_security()`'s new optional kwargs (`roc_6m`, `vol_20`, `vol_50`, `vol_ratio`, `roc_5`, `roc_20` — all additive, byte-identical defaults for every existing caller). `tests/test_train_meta_labelers.py::TestSixEligibleSignalsFeatureCompatibility` now proves all 6 signals' real, live `meta_label_features` pass `check_feature_compatibility()`. Disclosed plainly: this fixes ONLY the compatibility gate — DSR/PBO deployability is untouched and unaffected, and `cross_sectional_momentum` has a separate, pre-existing, still-open primary-signal-generation gap (documented in `ml/forecast_backfill.py`'s own "KNOWN GAP" docstring) that likely still prevents it from training a model at all, regardless of this fix.

- **Correction (2026-09-07, forecast-backfill 6-signal extension work): the `cross_sectional_momentum` "Signal column unconditionally NaN" claim in the bullet above is stale.** The "KNOWN GAP" docstring it referenced was removed from `ml/forecast_backfill.py` by an earlier fix (commit `8e000117`, "wire cross-sectional pre_compute into the forecast backfill pipeline") -- `step_3_generate_primary_signals` now detects this module's overridden `pre_compute` and routes it through `_run_cross_sectional_module`'s real two-phase replay (via `compute_batch_xsec`'s vectorized fast path). Verified directly: a synthetic-panel run trains all 4 horizons with a genuinely two-sided (+1/-1) Signal column. Of the 6 eligible signals, `sector_quality_rank` was ALSO unblocked by this same work (real point-in-time accrual/gross-profitability/sector data, see that signal's own bullet) and now genuinely trains. `vrp_premium_selling` remains genuinely blocked -- real historical implied volatility is structurally unavailable from FMP/Yahoo (see `docs/known_issues/vrp_premium_selling_no_historical_iv.md`); a separately-registered, quarantined `vrp_premium_selling_proxy` model_type trains against a realized-vol-derived proxy instead, structurally excluded from ever reaching live inference. `options_flow_sentiment` is DELIBERATELY never trained by the backfill screen either, on purpose, not for lack of data: its own legitimate live-production fallback (a price-momentum proxy fired only when no real UOA flow data exists) would otherwise fire unconditionally against the backfill's dummy context, training a meta-labeler on price momentum mislabeled as options-flow sentiment -- suppressed at the backfill dispatch point only (`ml/forecast_backfill.py::_run_cross_sectional_module`), with `signals/options_flow_sentiment.py`'s real live per-ticker behavior verified byte-identical/untouched. The webapp explainer text and `ml/meta_bootstrap.py`'s own comment were also corrected to stop claiming the feature-compatibility gate refuses all 6 signals -- it has passed for all 6 since the entry above.

- **"Today's Radar" — signal-based discovery feed (`pilots/radar_ranking.py`, `GET /signals/radar`, 2026-09)**: additive to the attribute-based Symbol Screener (`data/fmp_screener.py`, `GET /data/screener`) — Radar is signal-based discovery ("what is the model currently paying attention to, and why"), not attribute-based ("find me low-vol dividend names"). Zero new scoring logic: reads and ranks the SAME already-computed, already-persisted `Multifactor_Composite` cross-sectional z-score (`signals/multifactor.py::pre_compute()`, surfaced in `output/state_snapshot.json`'s per-signal dict as `multifactor_composite`) that `GET /symbols/{ticker}` already exposes per-ticker — see that field's own bullet above. `pilots/radar_ranking.py::radar_feed(snapshot, limit)` is a pure, dependency-light (stdlib + `typing` only — passes `tests/test_pilots_strategy_matrix.py::test_pilots_read_helpers_stay_dependency_light`'s auto-discovered allowlist with zero override needed) read helper mirroring `pilots/symbols.py::list_recommendations()`'s exact conventions (honest per-field nulling, deterministic tie-break, never raises). Two invariants worth stating explicitly, both confirmed against a real live `state_snapshot.json` during this feature's own verification pass: (1) **ranking is restricted to the snapshot's own `tickers[]` list, never the raw `signals[]` list** — `signals[]` can (and, confirmed live, does) carry a benchmark/proxy row (e.g. `SPY`) that `main_orchestrator.py::_write_state_snapshot` includes for macro-comparison purposes but that is NOT part of the tracked universe; ranking off `signals[]` directly would leak a non-tracked symbol into the feed. (2) **a symbol with no computed `multifactor_composite` this cycle is excluded entirely, never backfilled** — confirmed live, a real, non-trivial fraction of a cycle's universe can have this field null (missing fundamentals, a thinly-covered ticker, etc.), and the templated reason string independently null-checks each of the four sub-factor z-scores (`value_z`/`quality_z`/`lowvol_z`/`size_z`) before naming it — a symbol can carry a real composite while missing an individual sub-factor (also confirmed live), so "the composite exists" is never treated as proof every sub-factor does too. **`DailySignals` (the SQLite table `database_setup.py` creates from `config.COLUMN_SCHEMA`) was considered and rejected as this feature's data source** — see `docs/known_issues/daily_signals_missing_table.md`: the table is real but structurally, permanently empty (no live code path anywhere in this repo has ever written a row into it), so building Radar against it would either always render the empty state or tempt a fabricated demo fallback; `output/state_snapshot.json` is the genuinely live-populated source instead, the same one `pilots/observability.py`/`pilots/symbols.py`/`pilots/scoring.py` already read.
