Rendered as a "Today's Radar" panel embedded directly on the Marketplace screen (`webapp/src/screens/Marketplace.tsx`, tabKey `"pilots"`) — not a new screen/route, since the panel rides the existing screen's reachability — via a new `RadarCard.tsx` component; each card's server-templated `reason` string is rendered verbatim, never re-derived or paraphrased client-side. Clicking a card navigates to `/signals?symbol=<TICKER>`, read by a new mount-once `useSearchParams()` effect on `SignalBreakdown.tsx` (mirrors `PaperBroker.tsx`'s `?quickTradeSymbol=`/`Commands.tsx`'s `?builder=` precedent). `signals/credibility.py` (per-news-document credibility tiering, not a per-symbol daily aggregate) was evaluated and deliberately excluded from v1's reason string — a v2 enrichment, not required now. Tests: `tests/test_pilots_radar_ranking.py` (pure ranking/reason-string logic, empty/cold-start cases written first), extended `tests/test_pilots_api.py` (endpoint shape, auth tier, cold-start, limit validation), extended `webapp/src/screens/Marketplace.test.tsx` and `webapp/src/screens/SignalBreakdown.test.tsx`.

- **Universe Transparency panel (`webapp/src/components/UniverseCoverage.tsx`, 2026-09-07)**: a static composition panel showing three honest counts — Tracked universe, Forecast-covered, Full data coverage — composed client-side from the existing `GET /data/sync-report` response (no new backend endpoint). It correctly wires into existing data (`forecast_available`/`coverage`), needing no new API endpoint. The wider market stays reachable via a `<Link>` to Symbol Screener from the empty state. **Audit follow-up (2026-09-07, same day)**: an independent audit of the "Status: IMPLEMENTED" claim found four real gaps, all fixed in place. (1) The commit had recreated a 424-line `GEMINI.md` from scratch, byte-duplicating `CLAUDE.md` — the operator deliberately removed `GEMINI.md` a month earlier (`ce81e051`, folding its content into `AGENTS.md`) and no doc-sync convention for it exists (`.claude/hooks/sync_agent_docs.sh` mirrors only `CLAUDE.md`↔`AGENTS.md`); the wrongly-recreated file was deleted. (2) The new `GLOSSARY["universe coverage"]` entry and the parallel `docs/HOW_TO_GUIDE.md` prose both fabricated the mechanism behind "Forecast-covered," claiming it reflects "an explicit forecasting model configured in `sector_configs.json`" — that file is a per-SECTOR model/horizon backtest config (see its own field description in `settings.py`), unrelated to per-symbol coverage — when it actually reflects `ForecastTracker.get_covered_symbols()`: whether a price forecast was recorded for that symbol within the last 7 days. Both were corrected to describe the real mechanism. (3) "Full data coverage" was described as computed "on the latest run" (implying a cached pipeline artifact); it is actually a live probe performed when the screen loads (`data.portfolio_sync.build_sync_report`, recomputed fresh on every `GET /data/sync-report` call) — corrected in both places. (4) The plan's own §8 testing requirement ("three counts render correctly … including the case where all three numbers are equal and where they diverge") was unmet — the shipped test suite only exercised the filter-click UX, never asserted the badges' actual numeric values; two tests were added to `UniverseCoverage.test.tsx` covering a genuinely-diverging case against the real mock fixture (Tracked 8 / Forecast-covered 5 / Full coverage 3) and an all-equal synthetic fixture (3/3/3). **A second, more serious bug was found by a second independent audit pass of the same branch**: a sibling commit (`5eb9c6c1`) rewired `forecast_direction_arima_hw`'s `STRATEGY_REGISTRY` validation universe onto `compute_tracked_universe()`, but as shipped this *replaced* — rather than widened — the documented curated 10-ticker benchmark (`docs/signals/forecast_alignment.md`), making validated numbers non-reproducible across machines (verified live: a real cached Robinhood snapshot resolved to a 26-ticker REIT/BDC book with zero overlap with the curated list). Fixed via `FORECAST_DIRECTION_CURATED_UNIVERSE` — the universe is now the additive union, mirroring the 2026-08-21 tiered-universe-widening precedent (widen via union, never silently substitute) — plus a single `_resolve_registry_universe()` choke point resolving the two structural registry tests (`TestRegistryStructure`) this callable-universe change had broken. `forecast_direction_arima_hw` was re-validated for real (both yfinance and FMP confirmed reachable in this sandbox — no actual network outage): honest result both before and after is `deployable=False` (Sharpe -0.441→-0.175, DSR 0.145→0.338, MaxDD 38.3%→21.7% after the widening fix), documented in `docs/signals/forecast_alignment.md` and `docs/VALIDATION_STRATEGY_FIX_LOG.md`. That sibling fix does **not** touch `data/portfolio_sync.py::build_sync_report()`'s universe (the actual source this panel reads) or the live daily pipeline's forecasting universe, and does **not** close the 430-vs-26 forecast/trading universe mismatch this plan's §6 flags as separate and still open (`docs/known_issues/universe_count_reporting_mismatch.md`).

- **Signal Engine Vectorization**: As of Phase 4, `SignalAggregator`'s own aggregation step is natively vectorized in pandas/numpy (O(1) block computation) and row-based ticker iteration there has been removed. **Correction (2026-08 module efficiency audit):** not every `SignalModule` implementation is -- `SignalModule.compute_vectorized()` has a per-row `df.apply(axis=1)` fallback (`signals/base.py`) that a module silently inherits if it doesn't override the method, and as of the audit 7 of 20 registered modules do exactly that (`multifactor`, `cross_sectional_momentum`, `macro_regime`, `regime_multiplier`, `sector_quality_rank`, `lgbm_ranker`, `news_catalyst`). This is a debt-visibility gap, not an active performance emergency: per `docs/module_efficiency_redundancy_audit.md`'s F1, 5 of the 7 use the two-phase `pre_compute()` hook so their per-row `compute()` is a cheap dict lookup, and the other 2 are trivial conditionals -- `lgbm_ranker` in particular does NOT do per-row model inference despite that being the audit's own original (and wrong) assumption; its LightGBM prediction is already batched inside `pre_compute()`. `tests/test_no_iterrows_in_core_engines.py::test_registered_signal_module_overrides_compute_vectorized` now enumerates this list mechanically -- a new non-overriding module fails CI unless explicitly added there with a reason.

- **`OrchestratorDaemon` property-vs-method contract (`api/control_api.py`, `desktop/daemon_runtime.py`, 2026-07)**: `is_running` and `last_result` are `@property` on `OrchestratorDaemon` (read WITHOUT parens); `status()`/`get_run()`/`trigger_run()`/`set_interval()`/`start()`/`shutdown()` are methods — while `shared.orchestrator_runner.RunHandle.is_running()` **is** a method, and `api/control_api.py` calls both kinds. `POST /daemon/restart` shipped a 500 on exactly this confusion (`daemon.is_running()` evaluated the property to a bool then called it), reachable on every real deployment since a daemon is always attached there. Corollary for tests: a fake for a class with properties must be a hand-written class, **never** a bare `MagicMock` (every attribute on one is callable and truthy) and **not** `mock.create_autospec` either — `create_autospec` explicitly drops the spec for data descriptors and returns a *callable* `MagicMock` for a property, so it does not catch this class of bug. See `tests/test_control_api.py`'s `_FakeDaemon` plus its `test_daemon_properties_are_never_called_as_methods` (AST guard, derives the property set from the real class) and `test_fake_daemon_mirrors_orchestrator_daemon_member_kinds` (keeps the fake's shape from drifting from the real one).

- **`output/daemon.json` staleness fix — writer + reader, both required (`desktop/orchestrator_daemon.py`, `pilots/run_status.py`, `api/pilots_api.py`, 2026-07)**: `_write_daemon_file` was called exactly once, at startup, and never updated — a dead daemon left a file advertising `state: "running"` forever, which `GET /automation/status`'s `daemon_json` fallback (consulted precisely when the Control API is unreachable — the dead-daemon case) served as-is. **Writer half:** `_write_daemon_file` gained `state`/`started_at`/`stopped_at` kwargs (all defaulting to `None` = today's exact derived behavior) and is now called a SECOND time, with `state="stopped"`, as the last step of `_teardown()` — after `daemon.shutdown()` returns, with `started_at` threaded through from the real startup timestamp (never re-stamped to "now", which would fabricate a start time at the moment the daemon died — CONSTRAINT #4). **This only covers a graceful stop.** **Reader half — the load-bearing one:** a `SIGKILL`ed daemon can never perform that terminal write, so `pilots.run_status.read_daemon_json()` now also returns a derived `"pid_alive"` key (`True`/`False`/`None` via a private `_pid_alive(pid)` — `os.kill(pid, 0)`; rejects non-positive/`bool`/non-`int` pids before ever calling it, since pid 0 or negative targets a process group; `None` — never a fabricated `False` — when unknowable) added to the record rather than a second helper a caller could forget to call. Surfaced in `api/pilots_api.py`'s `GET /automation/status` (`daemon.pid_alive`, `None` on the `control_api` branch since no pid was ever probed there) and deliberately **not** used to null `GET /automation/schedule`'s `running_value`/`drift` for a dead pid — a dead daemon's last known interval is still the honest answer to "what was it running when it died"; `daemon.alive`/`daemon.pid_alive` already convey deadness. The file's own `state` string is deliberately never echoed directly in the API — it's an unverifiable self-report a `SIGKILL`ed process can never correct; `pid_alive` is the externally-verified signal. Webapp: `pid_alive: boolean | null` in `types.ts`'s `DaemonInfo`, mirrored in `mock.ts`'s fixture, rendered in `Settings.tsx` as three distinguishable states ("stopped — process not running" / "process alive, API not responding" / "last known state").

- **Shutdown-budget fix — one published total instead of three unreconciled hardcoded values (`settings.py`, `desktop/daemon_runtime.py`, `desktop/orchestrator_daemon.py`, `desktop/engine_supervisor.py`, 2026-07)**: `desktop/orchestrator_daemon.py`'s `_teardown()` used to hardcode a 5s Control-API join + 5s Pilots-API join + a `daemon.shutdown(timeout=10.0)` call that ITSELF hardcoded an *unbudgeted* extra 5s timer-thread join before its own 10s poll — nobody had added these up, and the actual total (20-25s) was routinely SIGKILLed by every parent supervisor at a flat 5-10s. New setting **`DAEMON_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`** (default `25.0` — the outer bound of every configuration reachable before this fix, so no path gets a shorter budget than it already had) is captured as ONE monotonic deadline at the top of `_teardown()` (inside its `_torn_down` idempotency guard, so a second no-op call never restarts the clock); each API-thread join is capped at `_SHUTDOWN_API_JOIN_SECONDS` (5.0, a module constant, not a setting) even when more of the deadline remains, and `daemon.shutdown()` receives whatever remains after both joins — correct because `OrchestratorDaemon.shutdown()` itself was fixed to budget its OWN timer-thread join *within* whatever `timeout` it's given (previously that join was a flat unbudgeted 5s on top). **Does NOT wait out an in-flight pipeline cycle** — a cycle can take minutes and there's no safe way to abort one mid-flight (see `pipeline/runner.py`'s own docstring); the budget only bounds the genuinely-bounded stages. `desktop/engine_supervisor.stop_engine`'s default timeout (used by `app_shell.py`'s only production call site) is now **backend-aware** via `getattr(handle, "mode", None)`: `"daemon"` → `DAEMON_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + 5.0` grace (so the parent always outwaits the child), anything else (incl. a handle missing `mode` entirely) → `5.0`, today's unchanged value for the `main.py --interval` backend, where a longer timeout would only delay giving up on a cycle that's genuinely mid-flight, never make shutdown more graceful. An explicit `timeout=` argument still always wins. **Required companion fix**: `reporting/state_snapshot.py`/`main_orchestrator.py`'s `state_snapshot.json` writers were the one place a "mid-cycle SIGKILL is safe" claim didn't hold — both used a bare `write_text()` (not write-then-rename), so a kill mid-write left a truncated file every reader treated as *missing* rather than merely stale; fixed to the same atomic idiom as `execution/kill_switch.py`/`desktop/orchestrator_daemon.py`'s `_write_daemon_file`. **Outer supervisors resized to exceed the new total** (published as a ladder in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §3.14): `launch_app.command`'s previous-instance-replace window (`SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS`, was a flat 10s → now 40s, with a progress line every ~5s so a genuinely long wait doesn't look like a hang); `scripts/com.investyo.stack.plist`'s `ExitTimeOut` (was unset/system-default → 45s). **Major related finding**: under launchd, graceful teardown was not actually running AT ALL before this fix — `AbandonProcessGroup` means launchd's SIGTERM targets the WRAPPER script's pid, not the daemon it ran in the foreground, and bash defers a trap's execution until the current foreground command completes (verified empirically), so the daemon never received that SIGTERM and was SIGKILLed outright at `ExitTimeOut`. Fixed by backgrounding the daemon in `scripts/investyo_stack_service.sh` and `wait`ing on it (interruptible), with an explicit trap that forwards the signal to the daemon and polls (bash 3.2 compatible — no `wait -n`) before reaping the two API children — see that script's own header comment for the full signal-path writeup. `deploy/investyo-daemon.service` gained explicit `TimeoutStopSec=45`/`KillSignal=SIGTERM` and `After=network-online.target` (was `network.target` — the daemon's first cycle makes outbound calls immediately). Two independent, pre-existing bugs found and fixed in the sibling systemd units while verifying this: `deploy/investyo-mcp.service` paired `Wants=network-online.target` with `After=network.target` — the classic ineffective combination (`Wants=` alone does no ordering); and **all three** units had `StartLimitIntervalSec`/`StartLimitBurst` inside `[Service]`, which silently no-ops on any systemd since v229 (2016) — moved to `[Unit]` in all three. **New env var:** `DAEMON_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (GUI-writable, `shared/env_io.py` `ALLOWED_KEYS` + `legacy/streamlit_command_center/panels/settings_manager.py` `_SETTINGS_LAYOUT`, non-secret — a GUI bug here can only make shutdown less graceful, never leak a credential or enable a dangerous action).

- **Multi-Model & Open Source AI Chat System (`api/data_api.py`, `settings.py`, `shared/env_io.py`, `webapp/src/components/AIChatInterface.tsx`, `webapp/src/api/client.ts`, 2026-08)**: Extended the platform's AI Assistant into a multi-model architecture capable of routing queries to Google Gemini (`gemini-2.5-flash`, `gemini-2.5-pro`), Anthropic Claude (`claude-3-5-sonnet`, `claude-3-5-haiku`, `claude-3-opus`), OpenAI ChatGPT (`gpt-4o`, `gpt-4o-mini`, `o1`, `o3-mini`), and Local / Open Source LLMs (Ollama, vLLM, DeepSeek, Llama-3, Qwen, Mistral, OpenRouter) via standard OpenAI-compatible endpoints (`LOCAL_LLM_BASE_URL`). Added `GET /data/ai/models` endpoint for dynamic provider/model catalog discovery, model selection bar in the UI drawer with custom model input, and automated fallback routing. Tests: `tests/test_data_api_chat.py`, `webapp/src/components/AIChatInterface.test.tsx`.

- **Gemini Live API WebSocket Streaming & Voice Controls (`api/ws_api.py`, `api/data_api.py`, `webapp/src/chat/useGeminiLive.ts`, `webapp/src/chat/audioStreamer.ts`, `webapp/src/components/AIChatInterface.tsx`, 2026-08)**: Added real-time, low-latency, bidirectional voice and text streaming over WebSockets between the Pilots PWA frontend and Google Gemini (`gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview`). Supported audio format is 16 kHz 16-bit linear PCM mono input (Web Audio microphone capture downsampled in browser) and 24 kHz 16-bit linear PCM mono output (streamed from Gemini and played via AudioContext with instant interruption support). Integrated dynamic read-only grounding tools (`list_all_pilots`, `get_pilot_holdings`, `get_pilot_recent_trades`, `get_current_portfolio`, `get_platform_status`) executed via `session.send_tool_response`. Gated by `AI_GENERATION_API_ENABLED`, `GEMINI_LIVE_CHAT_ENABLED`, and `STATE_API_TOKEN`. Tests: `tests/test_gemini_live_chat.py`, `webapp/src/chat/audioStreamer.test.ts`, `webapp/src/components/AIChatInterface.test.tsx`.

- **Execution audit trail wiring, circuit-breaker latency trip, and client-order-id collision fix (`execution/order_manager.py`, `execution/multi_broker_gateway.py`, `conftest.py`, 2026-08)**: three findings from an `execution/` audit, all fixed together (same root cause pattern: "implemented and tested, never verified end-to-end"). (1) `data/execution_audit_store.py::ExecutionAuditStore.record_audit`/`bulk_insert_audits` had zero production callers — the durable table backing `execution/sec_rule_606_reporter.py`'s SEC Rule 606 reporting was fully implemented and tested but never wired to a real order path, so `GET /pilots/execution/sec-606/report` always read an empty table (an honest all-zero result per CONSTRAINT #4, but disconnected end-to-end from real fills) — same bug class as the previously-confirmed `pilots/zero_dte_engine.py` 15:45 ET gate incident. Fixed via a new `OrderManager._record_execution_audit(intent, result)`, called from `submit_order_with_idempotency` on every real fill (`status != ERROR` and `filled_qty > 0`); injectable via a new constructor `audit_store=` param (lazily constructed against the default DB otherwise), best-effort/dead-letter-safe (never raises, matching `RunHistoryStore`'s pattern in `desktop/daemon_runtime.py`). NBBO bid/ask are left `None` (no generic NBBO source at this layer — `price_improvement` honestly computes to `0.0` rather than being fabricated). **Known limitation, disclosed not swept under the rug**: only covers a fill returned synchronously from `broker.submit_order` (true of `FMPPaperBroker`, the default paper broker); a broker whose fills arrive later via `stream_trade_updates` is not yet covered. **Required a new autouse test fixture**: `ExecutionAuditStore()`'s default constructor resolves to the real shared `~/.stockpy_local/quant_platform.db`, and ~15+ pre-existing test files construct `OrderManager(broker, ...)` directly with no `audit_store=` of their own — left unguarded, the suite would silently write real rows into an operator's production DB on every run. `conftest.py::_isolate_execution_audit_db_in_tests` (autouse, mirrors the existing `_isolate_validation_runs_db_in_tests` fixture for `ValidationHistoryStore` exactly) points the default resolver at `sqlite:///:memory:` for any test that doesn't pass its own explicit `db_url`/`sqlite_path` — **this is the pattern to follow** for any future store with an implicit default-DB constructor reachable from a widely-called production function. (2) `execution/multi_broker_gateway.py::CircuitBreaker`'s documented CLOSED→OPEN trip conditions ("consecutive failures >= threshold OR error rate >= threshold OR latency > threshold") had a dead third leg — `record_success` only logged a WARNING on a latency breach and never tripped, so a consistently-slow-but-never-erroring broker never actually tripped. Fixed via a self-contained `_consecutive_latency_breaches` counter on `CircuitBreaker` (no call-site changes needed elsewhere — every existing caller already passes `latency_ms`): reaching `config.max_consecutive_failures` consecutive breaches now trips it. Also removed the dead `CircuitBreakerConfig.auto_reset_on_heartbeat` field (referenced nowhere else in the codebase, implying a reset-bypass capability it never had) rather than inventing new behavior for an ambiguous, never-wired flag. (3) `execution/order_manager.py::make_client_order_id`'s canonicalization switched from a raw `f"{strategy_id}|{symbol}|..."` string to `json.dumps([...])` — the old scheme let a literal `|` inside `strategy_id`/`symbol` forge a collision between two distinct order tuples (reproduced: `('X', 'A|B', ...)` and `('X|A', 'B', ...)` produced byte-identical ids), which the idempotency dedup lock would then silently drop as "already submitted" — not reachable via any current call site (all pass fixed literals/validated tickers), but no longer possible for a future free-text caller to reintroduce. **(1) superseded 2026-09 (step 3c): the `OrderManager` audit hook, its `audit_store=` param, `GET /pilots/execution/sec-606/report` and the Paper Broker SEC 606 panel were removed; `conftest.py::_isolate_execution_audit_db_in_tests` stays until the store itself moves to `legacy/` in step 4.** Tests: `tests/test_multi_broker_gateway.py::test_circuit_breaker_latency_trip`, `tests/test_order_manager_idempotency.py::test_client_order_id_no_delimiter_collision`.

- **Options-Greeks dict-shape parity, cost-model market_cap threading, Almgren-Chriss well-posedness guard, and stress-threshold duplication fix (`pilots/options_risk.py`, `simulation_engine.py`, `investyo_mcp_server.py`, `execution/almgren_chriss_router.py`, `validation/stress_scenarios.py`, 2026-08)**: four small, independently-confirmed findings from an options-Greeks and execution-cost-model audit, none live-reachable today but each a real latent correctness/consistency risk. (1) `calculate_black_scholes_greeks`'s 0DTE branch (`t_years <= 1e-12`) omitted `rho`/`rho_1pct`/`rho_raw` while the degenerate-sigma and main computation branches both include them — a future caller doing `bs["rho"]` unconditionally on a 0DTE option would `KeyError`. Fixed by adding the three keys with the textbook-correct `rho=0.0` value for an expiring option, plus a regression test asserting all three return branches share an identical key set. (2) `simulation_engine.py`'s `optimize_strategy_vectorbt()`/`run_backtrader_simulation()` hardcoded `market_cap=None` at their `TieredCostModel` call sites, so `get_liquidity_tier(None)` always resolved `"large_cap"` regardless of the actual symbol — the liquidity-tier cost differentiation was defined but never exercised through this path. Both functions now accept an optional `market_cap` parameter (default `None`, byte-identical for any caller not yet passing one); the one real production caller, `investyo_mcp_server.py::run_backtest(symbol, period)`, now resolves a real market cap via `get_provider().get_fundamentals(symbol)["marketCap"]`, degrading to `None` (never a fabricated `0.0`, CONSTRAINT #4) on any fetch failure. `pairs/simulation.py`'s own separate `market_cap=None` call site is out of scope — a two-symbol pairs backtest has no single "the" market cap without a larger design decision. (3) `execution/almgren_chriss_router.py::compute_trading_trajectory` had no guard for the AC(2001) well-posedness condition (`eta_tilde = temp_impact - 0.5*perm_impact*tau > 0`) — a caller passing a `temp_impact` too small relative to `perm_impact*tau` would silently compute a negative "effective" temporary-impact coefficient and a nonsensical `expected_shortfall`. Not exercised by any current caller (verified against every test case and `api/pilots_api.py`'s fixed params), but now raises `ValueError` explicitly alongside the module's existing five validation checks. (4) `validation/stress_scenarios.py` defined its own local `MAX_STRESS_DRAWDOWN: float = 0.50` instead of importing `STRESS_MAX_DRAWDOWN` from `validation/thresholds.py`, even though `thresholds.py`'s own docstring already claims ownership of this value — two independent literals happened to agree (both `0.50`) but had no mechanism to stay that way. Fixed via a real import, preserving `MAX_STRESS_DRAWDOWN` as a valid public re-export (confirmed imported externally by `Gravity AI Review Suite.py` and `tests/test_stress_gate.py`). Tests: extended `tests/test_options_risk.py`, `tests/test_simulation_engine.py`, `tests/test_investyo_mcp_server.py::TestRunBacktest`, `tests/test_almgren_chriss_router.py`, `tests/test_stress_gate.py`.

- **Feature drift detection via PSI (`validation/covariate_drift.py`, `scripts/preflight_check.py`, 2026-08)**: added Population Stability Index (PSI) drift detection for model features to monitor if recent feature distributions diverge significantly from historical reference distributions. Gated by `FEATURE_DRIFT_PSI_ENABLED` in `settings.py`. Warns in `preflight_check.py` instead of blocking go-live.

- **Gravity suite**:
| Name | File | What it actually is |
|---|---|---|
| `GravityAIAuditor` (structural) | `Gravity AI Review Suite.py` | 90 real audit methods (`step_1`-`step_94`, gapped numbering), purely structural/Pandera/regex/backtest checks. **No LLM calls.** Can't be `import`ed (space in filename + top-of-file venv re-exec) — it imports other modules, nothing imports it. |
| `GravityAIAuditor` (grader) | `ai_verification_prompts.py:268` | A **different class, same name** — a keyword-based grader for text pasted back from an external LLM session. Not automated, not touched by this plan. |
| `gravity_ai_runner` | `engine/gravity_ai_runner.py` | The real one: Claude (primary) + Gemini (cross-checker) structured-output LLM auditor, 8 steps, gated `GRAVITY_AI_RUNNER_ENABLED` (default `False`), writes `output/gravity_ai_audit.json`. **Zero MCP tool touches this today** — Gap 2 fixes that. |

- **Per-Agent Identity & Least Privilege — scoped and closed as not-solvable-here (2026-09-07)**: an open item in `docs/AGENTIC_TRADING_SAFETY_FRAMEWORK.md`'s backlog asked for distinct identities/least-privilege scoping for Claude, Antigravity, and Jules. A dedicated research pass (`.claude/per_agent_identity_implementation_plan.md`) found the goal as framed is not achievable: Claude Code's and Antigravity's primary channel to this platform is a local stdio subprocess with full filesystem/shell access, not an HTTP call, so a per-agent token/identity layer over `api/auth.py`'s existing gates would not contain either — the same agent can always read any `.env` secret in cleartext or edit the enforcing check's own source rather than route through it. Every existing "hard" gate in this repo (kill-switch activation, `HIGH_STAKES_COMMANDS`'s `confirm` param, the `DANGEROUS_KEYS` typed-echo, the live-trade propose/approve split) reduces to the same shared-secret pattern; the one genuine exception (Robinhood's device-approval login) works only because it's a third party's channel this codebase has no access to. Operator confirmed **Path A**: document this finding and close the item rather than build a false sense of security — see `docs/AGENTIC_TRADING_SAFETY_FRAMEWORK.md`'s item 2 and open-gaps backlog for the full closure record. The real, achievable half of the original ask (durable cross-store attribution of which agent took an action) is being pursued separately via `.claude/cross_agent_audit_trail_implementation_plan.md`, unaffected by this closure. A smaller, genuinely valuable Path B (extending `mcp_oauth_store.py::OAuthUser`'s already-existing, currently-unconsumed `subject` claim for the *remote* `streamable-http`/OAuth MCP transport, where a caller does lack filesystem access) remains available as separate, deferred work if that surface ever sees active use.

- **Paper trading: real marks only + paper outcomes feed the models (`data/paper_account_store.py`, `pilots/price_provider.py`, `data/market_data.py`, 2026-09)**: five review findings fixed together. (1) Paper position marks no longer fabricate prices: stocks go through `CompositeProvider.get_quotes_batch` (cache + FMP→Alpaca→yfinance fallback) and a missing/zero/NaN quote is never a price (it previously marked a position at **$0**, and any FMP failure silently marked everything at cost). (2) Option marks come from real chain data only — intrinsic if expired, else bid/ask mid, else Black-Scholes on the contract's own live IV + `OPTIONS_RISK_FREE_RATE`, else last trade — replacing a hardcoded `sigma=0.30`/`r=0.04` that drove auto-exits; chains cached `PAPER_OPTION_MARK_CACHE_SECONDS` (60 s). An unpriced position falls back to cost basis but is **flagged** (`store.last_unpriced_symbols`, `PositionSnapshot.mark_is_estimated`); auto-exit evaluation skips such groups and the daemon's circuit-breaker loss-velocity sampler skips such ticks. (3) `PAPER_TRADES_BRIDGE_TO_TRANSACTIONS_ENABLED` now defaults **True** (operator decision) so paper outcomes reach `sizing.kelly`/`evaluation_engine` — but only signal-driven EQUITY trades: option contracts and `PAPER_TRADES_BRIDGE_EXCLUDED_STRATEGIES` (default `["Manual Trade", "Delta Hedge", "untagged"]`) are recorded as `bridge_status="excluded"`, because production Kelly reads the `trades` ledger unfiltered by strategy. That ledger still has no paper/live column (bridged rows carry `notes="Paper bridge, ..."`), so MCP/reporting consumers now include paper outcomes. (4) `pilots.price_provider.get_latest_prices` routes through the cached batch provider, cutting the 1 Hz `/ws/risk/portfolio` stream from two uncached FMP calls/sec/client to at most one per quote-TTL window. (5) `CompositeProvider.get_quotes_batch`'s per-symbol fallback skips FMP after a WHOLE-batch failure instead of re-hitting it N times. Tests: `tests/test_paper_marking_and_model_feed.py`, extended `tests/test_market_data.py`, `tests/test_price_provider.py`, `tests/test_daemon_runtime.py`, `webapp/src/screens/RetrospectiveJournalModelFeed.test.tsx`; a root `conftest.py` autouse fixture (`_stub_paper_marking_network_in_tests`, opt out with `@pytest.mark.real_paper_marking`) keeps paper marking network-free in tests. New env vars: `PAPER_TRADES_BRIDGE_EXCLUDED_STRATEGIES`, `PAPER_OPTION_MARK_CACHE_SECONDS`. **Follow-up, same PR — no fixed volatility left in paper options**: `data/paper_account_store.py` now exposes `resolve_option_iv()` and `resolve_option_price_per_share()` (intrinsic if expired → side-aware live quote: buy at ask / sell at bid → mid → Black-Scholes on the contract's OWN live IV → last trade; `prefer_model=True` for what-if spots). `pilots/options_risk.py::calculate_position_greeks` has no default `sigma` any more (was 0.25): `calculate_portfolio_greeks` resolves each leg's live chain IV, and a leg without one (or with an unparseable expiry, which used to assume 30 days) is `missing_data` with a `missing_reason` and is excluded from aggregates. `execution/options_paper_executor.py`'s `_price_option_contract` (σ=0.30 fills, plus a σ=0.20 post-earnings close and an `entry_price * iv_crush_factor` fallback) is replaced by `_real_option_price_per_contract`. Strike-built earnings-crush entries and `settle_post_earnings_trades` closes fill at real side-aware prices or refuse/skip with a reason (never a partial close). `iv_crush_factor` is kept only for call compatibility and is unused. Still open: `pilots/scenario_matrix.py`'s `base_iv=0.25` default.

- **Retrospective Learning Loop & Trade Autopsy Engine (`pilots/retrospective_composer.py`, `pilots/retrospective_narrative.py`, `pilots/retrospective_insights.py`, `data/paper_account_store.py`, `api/pilots_api.py`, `webapp/src/screens/RetrospectiveJournal.tsx`, 2026-09, Milestones 1–5; 5-agent audit + fix pass same month)**: An end-to-end post-trade autopsy, conviction calibration, and systemic pattern analytics loop connecting realized paper trade outcomes back to the exact quantitative context at entry.
  - **As originally shipped, an independent 5-agent audit found the feature contained more real fabrication/correctness risk than its own "VICTORY CONFIRMED" walkthrough claimed** — every finding below was fixed in the same pass, not merely disclosed. Two were genuinely critical: (1) this module's own "E2E acceptance" test file (72 of its tests) passed IDENTICALLY when the three production modules were made unimportable, because its verification-critical WP-C/WP-G tests called production functions with NO store/data argument and silently fell back to hand-built fixtures or to production code that walked the call stack looking for a caller-local variable by name (`sys._getframe()`, since deleted from both `pilots/retrospective_composer.py` and `pilots/retrospective_insights.py`) — a real cross-database-leak hazard in production, not just a test smell. (2) `api/pilots_api.py` had grown a LOCAL override shadowing the real, imported `api.auth.require_read_token`, adding a `host == "testclient"` bypass that silently defeated the upstream fail-closed-when-non-loopback 503 across **all 111** `require_read_token`-gated endpoints in this file, not just the retrospective ones — reverted to the plain upstream import; the 3 tests that needed the bypass now use `TestClient(app, client=("127.0.0.1", ...))`, this file's own pre-existing convention.
  - **Forward-Only Entry Snapshot Capture (`data/paper_account_store.py`)**: `paper_entry_snapshots` captures point-in-time state (entry price, model conviction, regime, macro indicators, top signal contributions, raw score components) forward-only across `apply_fill`, `apply_multi_leg_fill`, and `apply_roll_fill`. Strictly enforces anti-fabrication: legacy trades lacking entry snapshots are stamped with `entry_snapshot_id=None` and report `decision_context_status="not_captured"` — never pseudo-reconstructed. **Audit finding, fixed**: `_create_entry_snapshot`'s provenance-inference fallback (when a caller omits `provenance=`) is CORRECT for the one case its `strategy_id == "Manual Trade"` string-match covers, but no real production writer ever explicitly passes `provenance=`/`conviction=`/`macro_regime=` — meaning every automated writer that DOES pass a real `pilot_id` (the dedicated pilot writers: `earnings_crush.py`, `dispersion_trading.py`, `copula_stat_arb.py`, `zero_dte_engine.py`) got its provenance INFERRED as `"signal_driven"` from ID presence alone, not genuinely captured, while the GENERIC options auto-scan (whose directives never carry a `pilot_id` at all) gets no snapshot captured whatsoever — a real, disclosed feature-completeness gap (the auto-scan path stays honestly "not captured," never fabricated, but the feature is currently inert for that majority-volume path). Separately, `pilots/earnings_crush.py`'s own exception-recovery fallback (pre-existing, from PR before this feature) reused the generic `execute_paper_order` "Manual Trade" executor for an AUTOMATED trade recovering from a primary-path failure, silently misclassifying it as manual — `execute_paper_order`/`pilots.paper_broker.execute_paper_order` now accept optional `strategy_id`/`pilot_id`/`provenance` overrides (default preserves the exact original "Manual Trade" behavior for the real Quick Trade/Options Chain caller), and `earnings_crush.py`'s fallback passes `strategy_id="earnings-crush", pilot_id="earnings-crush", provenance="signal_driven"` explicitly.
  - **Atomic Transaction Bridge & Telemetry**: Extends `paper_closed_trades` with `entry_snapshot_id`, `bridge_status` (`"bridged"` | `"failed"` | `"disabled"` | `"not_attempted"`), `bridged_trade_id`, `bridge_error`, and `bridged_at`. Bridges closed paper trades to `TransactionsStore.record_trade()` inside an isolated SAVEPOINT (`session.begin_nested()`), forwarding model conviction to preserve the statistical link. Order execution fails open: a bridge failure logs a warning, stamps `bridge_status="failed"` and `bridge_error`, but never aborts the trade fill. `PaperAccountStore.get_bridge_completeness_metrics()` surfaces bridge telemetry (`attempted_count`, `bridged_count`, `failed_count`, `disabled_count`, `completeness_pct`). **Audit finding, fixed (CONSTRAINT #4/#6)**: the table-missing / query-error / bridge-enabled-but-nothing-yet-attempted branches previously all reported a fabricated `completeness_pct=100.0`/`status="healthy"` — exactly the false all-clear this metric exists to catch (WP-D). Now `completeness_pct=None`/`status="unknown"` in every genuinely-unmeasured case; `100.0`/`"disabled"` is preserved ONLY for the one case where it's honest — the bridge deliberately turned off, so there is truly nothing to bridge. The batch-insights local fallback (below) mirrors the same fix, plus a denominator bug (`bridged_count / total_records` instead of `bridged_count / attempted_count`, which silently diluted a real failure rate whenever any trade landed in the disabled bucket).
  - **Per-Trade Autopsy Composer (`pilots/retrospective_composer.py`)**: Builds `RetrospectiveTradeRecord` joining execution realization, entry context, model calibration, and excursion metrics via `evaluation_engine.EvaluationEngine.evaluate_portfolio()`'s real MAE/MFE/Edge Ratio math against an isolated single-trade in-memory `TransactionsStore("sqlite:///:memory:")`, eliminating multi-trade excursion collisions. Calibrates conviction against `evaluation_engine.calibration_curve()`'s real conviction-bin win rates, honestly reporting `"not_applicable"` for manual/unrecorded/no-conviction trades and `"insufficient_sample"` when a real lookup found a bin with `N < 5`. **Audit finding, fixed (CRITICAL)**: the isolated single-trade evaluation previously injected its throwaway store into `evaluate_portfolio()` via `unittest.mock.patch("transactions_store.TransactionsStore", ...)` — a PROCESS-GLOBAL monkeypatch, reachable from a synchronous FastAPI endpoint dispatched to Starlette's worker threadpool, that could leak one concurrent request's throwaway trade into an unrelated request's real read (bystander poisoning), permanently corrupt the module namespace on a non-LIFO patch exit, or defeat its own isolation purpose entirely when a caller's OWN test fixture had globally patched `TransactionsStore.__init__` (proven: this exact circularity let the shipped WP-E "byte-for-byte parity" tests pass unchanged through a deliberately side-flipped, price-doubled, or hold-window-relocated composer reconstruction). Fixed via a new, explicit `transactions_store=` dependency-injection parameter on `evaluate_portfolio()` itself — the composer now passes its isolated store directly, no monkeypatching anywhere in this module. The `record_trade`/`close_trade` calls building that isolated store were also moved inside the enclosing `try`, so a genuine construction failure dead-letters into the honest "evaluation data unavailable" record instead of propagating as an uncaught 500. The stack-frame-walking store-resolution fallback (see the E2E-acceptance-suite item above) was deleted from `compose_trade_retrospective` too. A `DualStatusStr` custom string subclass — reconciling two spellings (`"not captured"`/`"not_captured"`) the module's own test suite disagreed with itself on — was removed in favor of ONE canonical underscore-form vocabulary (`STATUS_NOT_CAPTURED`/`STATUS_NOT_APPLICABLE`/`STATUS_INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE`/`STATUS_AVAILABLE`), matching `provenance`/`bridge_status`'s existing convention and what `webapp/src/api/types.ts` actually declares; the class's cross-format `__eq__` also violated the `__eq__`/`__hash__` invariant and evaporated at the JSON wire boundary regardless. The not-applicable calibration branch now reports `bin_trade_count=None` (never a fabricated `0`, which the narrative previously rendered as a false "N=0 < 5, insufficient sample" measurement for a trade whose calibration was never even attempted).
  - **Deterministic Rule-Based Narrative Generator (`pilots/retrospective_narrative.py`)**: A 3-clause (Entry, Outcome, Excursion & Calibration) plain-prose sentence builder — no markdown sections, no external LLM. **Audit findings, all fixed**: (1) the "percentage return unavailable due to degenerate entry price" clause fired whenever a percentage was merely absent for ANY reason, asserting a specific unverified cause (sometimes self-contradicting an adjacent clause); now gated on the entry price genuinely being `<= 0`, with a neutral "percentage return unrecorded" wording otherwise. (2) A signal-driven trade's "entry conviction binned at historical N% win rate" claim never checked that a conviction was actually present — could name a bin placement for a trade with no captured conviction at all; now requires `conviction is not None`, with an explicit "conviction not captured, calibration unavailable" clause otherwise. (3) A signal-driven trade with a genuine conviction but no calibration bin data (e.g. a calibration-engine error) previously fell through to complete silence about calibration; now states "historical calibration unavailable (no calibration data)" explicitly. (4) Free-text fields (`operator_notes`, `strategy_id`) are now sanitized (control characters/newlines collapsed, length-capped) before interpolation, and the quoted operator note additionally has embedded `"` characters neutralized, closing a quote-breaking injection that let a crafted note visually fabricate additional "measured" system sentences following it. (5) The final None/NaN/null defense-in-depth cleanup regex is now applied ONLY to this module's own system-authored text — the operator's quoted note is spliced back in as the very last step via a sentinel placeholder, so a genuine note reading e.g. "null hypothesis rejected" is never silently rewritten to "unrecorded hypothesis rejected."
  - **Systemic Pattern Analytics & Strict Cohort Isolation (`pilots/retrospective_insights.py`)**: `generate_batch_retrospective_insights` computes pattern analytics with strict cohort isolation: automated/manual/unrecorded trades are partitioned into independent cohorts with zero top-level blended metrics. **Audit findings, all fixed**: (1) a missing/unmeasurable `realized_pnl` was previously coerced to a fabricated `0.0`, silently counting an unmeasurable trade as a measured breakeven and diluting win rate/total PnL/Brier score for the whole cohort — now returns `None` and is excluded from every PnL-based aggregate, with the exclusion count reported honestly (`excluded_unmeasurable_pnl_count`). (2) The contrastive-insights excursion comparison asserted an unearned causal claim ("indicating wider loss tolerance or delayed stop execution") from comparing two means with no sample floor — now gated on a minimum of 5 trades in EACH cohort, states measurement only (no causal attribution), and always discloses N for every comparison. (3) `signal_driven_cohort` was the exact same dict object as `automated_cohort` (a caller mutating one silently mutated the other) — now a genuine copy. (4) See the bridge-telemetry item above for this module's own local `bridge_health` fallback fix (same `completeness_pct`/denominator bugs, fixed identically).
  - **FastAPI Read Endpoints (`api/pilots_api.py`)**: Gated with fail-open `require_read_token` (`STATE_API_TOKEN`, the genuine upstream `api.auth` implementation — see the audit-finding note at the top of this bullet): `GET /pilots/paper-broker/trades/{trade_id}/retrospective` (returns 404 for unknown trade, 422 for invalid id), `GET /pilots/paper-broker/retrospective/insights` (supports `limit` [1, 1000], `symbol`, `strategy_id`), and `GET /pilots/paper-broker/bridge/metrics`.
  - **Pilots PWA Retrospective Journal (`webapp/src/screens/RetrospectiveJournal.tsx`, `RetrospectiveDetailModal.tsx`)**: Mounted at `/retrospective` (`section: "trading"`, icon `History` 📜), cross-linked from `TradingHub.tsx` and `PaperBroker.tsx` Closed Trades table. Features a real-time bridge completeness banner, Trade Journal tab with multi-filter bar and excursion autopsies, wide detail modal with rule-based narrative, excursion grid, decision context, and calibration bin placement, and Pattern Insights tab with isolated cohort cards. **Audit findings, all fixed**: (1) `webapp/src/api/types.ts` declared several fields the backend has never emitted (`RetrospectiveEvaluationStatus`'s `"bridge_unavailable"`/`"bars_unavailable"`/`"pending"`; `RetrospectiveCalibration.status`'s implicit `"scored"`) and a wrong field name (`operator_notes` — the real wire key is `decision_rationale`) — the `"scored"` mismatch meant the calibration panel NEVER rendered real measured data on a live response, always falling to "not applicable" regardless of a genuinely calibrated trade; both files now declare and read the real wire contract. (2) MAE/MFE are fractions of entry price (`evaluation_engine.py`'s contract, always a positive magnitude for MAE), not dollar amounts — both screens rendered them as raw `$X.XX`; now formatted as percentages. (3) `bin_range` is a real `[number, number]` tuple, not a pre-formatted string — a prior fix of item (1) alone would have exposed a raw `[0.8, 0.9]` rendering as the literal text "0.80.9"; now formatted explicitly. (4) `bin_trade_count ?? 0` fabricated a sample size for a trade whose calibration was never attempted (composer now emits `None` there, per the composer fix above) — now renders "—" instead of a fabricated "0 trades". (5) `RetrospectiveJournal.tsx` independently reimplemented the exact `strategy_id`-presence provenance-inference anti-pattern the backend explicitly forbids, for any trade whose composed record hadn't loaded yet — replaced with an honest "unknown" default. (6) Several `?? 0`/ternary-without-null-branch color fallbacks painted a missing value the same color as a real bad measurement (a null PnL% as green, a null win rate as red, an unmeasured bridge completeness as alarmingly red) — all now render a neutral/muted color when the underlying value is `null`. (7) The manual-trade decision-context panel collapsed "manual" and "unknown" provenance into one branch, asserting "no algorithmic signal was evaluated at entry" even for a trade whose provenance is genuinely unrecorded — now a proper 3-way branch. Mock mode (`webapp/src/api/mock.ts`) fixtures were corrected to match the real contract throughout (fraction-scale MAE/MFE, `decision_rationale`, tuple `bin_range`, real calibration status strings, `null` `bin_trade_count` where genuinely not applicable).
  - **Known, disclosed follow-ups (not fabrication risks, feature-completeness gaps)**: the generic options auto-scan path (the majority of automated paper-trade volume) still captures no entry snapshot at all, since its directives never carry a `pilot_id` — wiring real conviction/macro-regime context through that path is a larger, separate follow-up; `mock.ts` still has no dedicated "genuinely empty" cohort-insights fixture (a zero-trade cold start currently reuses the happy-path numbers rather than an honestly-zeroed one).
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_retrospective_composer.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_narrative.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_cohort_insights.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_schema_and_bridge.py`, `tests/test_pilots_retrospective_api.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_adversarial_m1.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_adversarial_m3.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_adversarial_m6_2.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_learning_loop_challenger_m6.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_learning_loop_m1_challenger2.py`, `tests/test_retrospective_e2e_acceptance.py`, `tests/test_challenger_m4_adversarial.py` (340 tests, independently re-run and verified passing after the audit fixes — real-count correction of the original walkthrough's unverified "383" claim), plus `webapp/src/components/RetrospectiveM4Adversarial.test.tsx` (9 tests covering both `RetrospectiveJournal.tsx` and `RetrospectiveDetailModal.tsx` — the ONLY dedicated test coverage for either component; the walkthrough's claimed `RetrospectiveJournal.test.tsx`/`RetrospectiveDetailModal.test.tsx` files were never actually created).
